UC01 Login

UC ID and Name:UC01 LoginCreated By:DuyQKCE182413Date Created:26/01/2026Primary Actor:Customer, Staff, Canteen Owner, Admin SystemSecondary Actors:NoneTrigger:The User wants to log in to the system to use UniLife features.Description:This Use Case allows the User to log in to the system using their registered email and password. After successful login, the User will receive an access token to authenticate subsequent requests.Preconditions:? The login page is displayed.
? The User has an account in the system.Postconditions:? The User is authenticated and logged into the system.
? The access token is saved to localStorage.
? User information is saved to localStorage.
? The User is redirected to the home page.Normal Flow:1. The User accesses the login page.
2. The User enters email in the "Email" field.
3. The User enters password in the "Password" field.
4. The User clicks the "Login" button.
5. The system validates the login credentials.
6. The system generates an access token and returns user information.
7. The system displays message "Successful login".
8. The system redirects the user to the home page.Alternative Flows:A. Incorrect email or password (Step 5)
A.1 The system cannot find user with the email or password does not match.
A.2 The system displays message "Incorrect email or password".
A.3 The User can re-enter information and try again.
A.4 Return to Step 2.
B. Missing login information (Step 4)
B.1 The User has not entered email or password.
B.2 The system displays message requesting complete information.
B.3 Return to Step 2.Exceptions:E. Network connection lost (Step 5)
E.1 Network connection is interrupted while sending login request.
E.2 The system displays network error message.
E.3 End Use Case.
F. Unknown system error (Step 5 or Step 6)
F.1 An unexpected system error occurs.
F.2 The system logs the error.
F.3 The system displays generic error message.
F.4 End Use Case.Priority:High, Must HaveFrequency of Use:Very frequentBusiness Rules:? Email must be in valid email format.
? Password must be at least 6 characters.
? Users registered with Google cannot login with password.Other Information:? The system supports "Ghi nh? ??ng nh?p" option.
? Can alternatively log in with Google.Assumptions:? The User has stable internet connection.
UC02 Register

UC ID and Name:UC02 RegisterCreated By:DuyQKCE182413Date Created:26/01/2026Primary Actor:GuestSecondary Actors:Email ServiceTrigger:The Guest wants to create a new account to use the UniLife system.Description:This Use Case allows the Guest to register a new account in the system. The registration process includes: entering personal information, sending an OTP code via email, and verifying the OTP to complete registration.Preconditions:? The registration page is displayed.
? The Guest has a valid and active email.
? The Guest does not have an account in the system.Postconditions:? A new account is created in the system with role "customer".
? Email is verified (emailVerified = true).
? Access token is created and saved.
? The User is redirected to the login page.Normal Flow:1. The Guest accesses the registration page.
2. The Guest enters full name in the "H? và tên" field.
3. The Guest enters email in the "Email" field.
4. The Guest enters phone number in the "S? ?i?n tho?i" field.
5. The Guest enters password in the "M?t kh?u" field.
6. The Guest clicks the "??ng ký" button.
7. The system validates the data.
8. The system checks that email and phone number do not exist.
9. The system generates a 6-digit OTP code and sends it to the email.
10. The system displays message "?ã g?i mã OTP!".
11. The system redirects to the OTP verification page.
12. The Guest enters the 6-digit OTP code received via email.
13. The system verifies the OTP code.
14. The system creates a new account.
15. The system displays message "??ng ký thành công!".
16. The system redirects to the login page.Alternative Flows: Flows	A. Email already in use (Step 8)
A.1 The system detects that the email already exists in the system.
A.2 The system displays message "Email ?ã ???c s? d?ng".
A.3 The Guest can edit the email and try again.
A.4 Return to Step 3.
B. Phone number already in use (Step 8)
B.1 The system detects that the phone number already exists.
B.2 The system displays message "S? ?i?n tho?i ?ã ???c s? d?ng".
B.3 The Guest can edit the phone number and try again.
B.4 Return to Step 4.
C. Invalid OTP code (Step 13)
C.1 The entered OTP code is incorrect or has expired.
C.2 The system displays message "Xác th?c OTP th?t b?i".
C.3 The system clears the entered OTP code.
C.4 The Guest can re-enter the OTP code.
C.5 Return to Step 12.
D. Resend OTP code (Step 12)
D.1 The Guest clicks "G?i l?i mã OTP" after countdown ends (60 seconds).
D.2 The system generates a new OTP code and sends it to the email.
D.3 The system resets the countdown to 60 seconds.
D.4 Return to Step 12.
E. Password not long enough (Step 7)
E.1 The entered password is less than 6 characters.
E.2 The system displays message "M?t kh?u ph?i có ít nh?t 6 ký t?".
E.3 Return to Step 5.
F. Guest already has account (Step 1)
F.1 The Guest clicks "??ng nh?p ngay" link.
F.2 The system redirects to the login page.
F.3 End Use Case.Exceptions:E. Unable to send OTP email (Step 9)
E.1 The system encounters an error when sending OTP email.
E.2 The system displays message "Không th? g?i email OTP. Vui lòng th? l?i sau".
E.3 End Use Case.
F. Rate limit exceeded (Step 9)
F.1 The Guest sends too many OTP requests in a short time.
F.2 The system displays message "Vui lòng ??i X giây tr??c khi g?i l?i mã OTP".
F.3 The Guest must wait for the cooldown period.
F.4 Return to Step 6.
G. Network connection lost (Step 6, Step 12)
G.1 Network connection is interrupted.
G.2 The system displays network error message.
G.3 End Use Case.
H. Unknown system error
H.1 An unexpected system error occurs.
H.2 The system logs the error.
H.3 The system displays generic error message.
H.4 End Use Case.Priority:High, Must HaveFrequency of Use:ModerateBusiness Rules:? Email must be in valid format and not exist in the system.
? Phone number must not exist in the system.
? Password must be at least 6 characters.
? OTP code is valid for 5 minutes.
? OTP code consists of 6 digits.
? Must wait 60 seconds between OTP sends.Other Information:? Default role for new account is "customer".
? Supports pasting OTP code from clipboard.Assumptions:? The Guest has access to email to receive OTP.
? Email service is functioning normally.
UC03 Login with Google

UC ID and Name:UC03 Login with GoogleCreated By:DuyQKCE182413Date Created:26/01/2026Primary Actor:Guest, CustomerSecondary Actors:Google OAuth ServiceTrigger:The User/Guest wants to quickly log in or register using a Google account.Description:This Use Case allows the User/Guest to log in to the system using a Google account. If the User does not have an account, the system will automatically create a new account based on information from Google. If the User already has an account (registered with email), the system will link it with Google.Preconditions:? The login page is displayed.
? The User/Guest has a valid Google account.
? Google OAuth is properly configured in the system.Postconditions:? The User is authenticated and logged into the system.
? The access token is saved to localStorage.
? User information is saved to localStorage.
? If new user, account is created with provider = "google".
? The User is redirected to the home page.Normal Flow:1. The User accesses the login page.
2. The User clicks the "??ng nh?p b?ng Google" button.
3. The system displays Google OAuth popup.
4. The User selects a Google account to log in.
5. Google authenticates and returns ID Token.
6. The system sends ID Token to backend for verification.
7. The system verifies the Google ID Token.
8. The system checks the user in the database.
9. The system generates an access token and returns user information.
10. The system displays message "??ng nh?p thành công!".
11. The system redirects the user to the home page.Alternative Flows:A. New user - No account (Step 8)
A.1 The system cannot find user with email from Google.
A.2 The system creates a new account with information from Google (email, name, avatar).
A.3 The system sets provider = "google", emailVerified = true.
A.4 Continue to Step 9.
B. User has local account (Step 8)
B.1 The system finds user registered with email/password.
B.2 The system links account with Google (update provider = "google").
B.3 The system updates avatar if not set.
B.4 Continue to Step 9.

C. User previously logged in with Google (Step 8)
C.1 The system finds user with provider = "google".
C.2 The system updates lastLoginAt.
C.3 Continue to Step 9.
D. User cancels Google login (Step 4)
D.1 The User closes popup or clicks Cancel.
D.2 The system displays message "Không th? k?t n?i v?i Google. Vui lòng th? l?i."
D.3 End Use Case.Exceptions:E. Invalid Google ID Token (Step 7)
E.1 Token from Google cannot be verified.
E.2 The system displays message "Invalid Google token".
E.3 End Use Case.
F. Google does not provide email (Step 7)
F.1 Token does not contain email information.
F.2 The system displays message "Email not provided by Google".
F.3 End Use Case.
G. Network connection lost (Step 3, Step 6)
G.1 Network connection is interrupted.
G.2 The system displays network error message.
G.3 End Use Case.
H. Unknown system error
H.1 An unexpected system error occurs.
H.2 The system logs the error.
H.3 The system displays generic error message.
H.4 End Use Case.Priority:High, Should HaveFrequency of Use:FrequentBusiness Rules:? Email from Google is automatically verified (emailVerified = true).
? Google account will not have password (password = null).
? Default role is "customer" for new accounts.
? Display name is taken from Google profile or created from email.Other Information:? Avatar is taken from Google profile if user doesn't have one.
? OTP verification is not required when registering with Google.Assumptions:? Google OAuth service is functioning normally.
? The User allows the application to access basic information.
UC04 Logout

UC ID and Name:UC04 LogoutCreated By:DuyQKCE182413Date Created:26/01/2026Primary Actor:All userSecondary Actors:NoneTrigger:The User wants to log out of the system.Description:This Use Case allows the User to log out of the system. The current access token will be invalidated (blacklisted) and user data will be removed from localStorage.Preconditions:? The User is logged into the system.
? A valid access token is stored in localStorage.Postconditions:? The access token is added to the blacklist.
? The access token is removed from localStorage.
? User information is removed from localStorage.
? The User is redirected to the login page.Normal Flow:1. The User clicks on the account menu or logout button.
2. The User confirms wanting to log out.
3. The system sends logout request to backend.
4. Backend adds the access token to blacklist.
5. The system removes the access token from localStorage.
6. The system removes user information from localStorage.
7. The system redirects the user to the login page.Alternative Flows:A. Logout API fails (Step 3)
A.1 Logout request to backend is unsuccessful.
A.2 The system still continues to clear local data.
A.3 Continue to Step 5.Exceptions:E. Network connection lost (Step 3)
E.1 Network connection is interrupted.
E.2 The system still clears local data and logs out user.
E.3 Continue to Step 5.
F. Unknown system error (Step 3)
F.1 An unexpected system error occurs.
F.2 The system logs the error.
F.3 The system still continues to log out on client side.
F.4 Continue to Step 5.Priority:High, Must HaveFrequency of Use:ModerateBusiness Rules:? Blacklisted tokens cannot be reused for authentication.
? Local data is always cleared even if API has errors.Other Information:? Token blacklist can use Redis in production.
? Currently uses in-memory Set for blacklist.Assumptions:? The User has access to the logout button when logged in.
UC05 Forgot Password

UC ID and Name:UC05 Forgot PasswordCreated By:DuyQKCE182413Date Created:26/01/2026Primary Actor:All usersSecondary Actors:Email ServiceTrigger:The User forgot their password and wants to reset to a new password.Description:This Use Case allows the User to reset their password when forgotten. The process includes: entering email, receiving OTP code via email, verifying OTP, and entering new password.Preconditions:? The forgot password page is displayed.
? The User has an account in the system with provider = "local".
? The User has access to the registered email.
? Postconditions:? The User's password is updated to the new password.
? The User can log in with the new password.
? The User is redirected to the login page.Normal Flow:1. The User accesses the forgot password page (from the "Quên m?t kh?u?" link on the login page).
2. The User enters email in the "Email" field.
3. The User clicks the "G?i mã xác th?c" button.
4. The system checks that the email exists in the system.
5. The system generates a 6-digit OTP code and sends it to the email.
6. The system displays message "?ã g?i mã OTP!".
7. The system redirects to the forgot password OTP verification page.
8. The User enters the 6-digit OTP code received via email.
9. The system verifies the OTP code.
10. The system generates a reset token (valid for 10 minutes).
11. The system displays message "Xác th?c thành công!".
12. The system redirects to the reset password page.
13. The User enters new password in the "M?t kh?u m?i" field.
14. The User enters new password confirmation in the "Xác nh?n m?t kh?u" field.
15. The User clicks the "??t l?i m?t kh?u" button.
16. The system validates the reset token.
17. The system updates the new password.
18. The system displays message "??t l?i m?t kh?u thành công!".
19. The system redirects the user to the login page.Alternative Flows:A. Email does not exist (Step 4)
A.1 The system cannot find user with the entered email.
A.2 The system displays message "Email không t?n t?i trong h? th?ng".
A.3 Return to Step 2.
B. Account registered with Google (Step 4)
B.1 The system detects account registered with Google/Social.
B.2 The system displays message "Tài kho?n này ??ng ký b?ng Google. Vui lòng ??ng nh?p b?ng Google".
B.3 End Use Case.
C. Invalid OTP code (Step 9)
C.1 The entered OTP code is incorrect or has expired.
C.2 The system displays message "Xác th?c OTP th?t b?i".
C.3 The system clears the entered OTP code.
C.4 The User can re-enter the OTP code.
C.5 Return to Step 8.
D. Resend OTP code (Step 8)
D.1 The User clicks "G?i l?i mã OTP" after countdown ends (60 seconds).
D.2 The system generates a new OTP code and sends it to the email.
D.3 The system resets the countdown to 60 seconds.
D.4 Return to Step 8.
E. Password not long enough (Step 15)
E.1 The new password is less than 6 characters.
E.2 The system displays message "M?t kh?u ph?i có ít nh?t 6 ký t?".
E.3 Return to Step 13.
F. Password confirmation does not match (Step 15)
F.1 The password confirmation does not match the new password.
F.2 The system displays message "M?t kh?u xác nh?n không kh?p".
F.3 Return to Step 14.
G. Reset token expired (Step 16)
G.1 Reset token has expired (over 10 minutes).
G.2 The system displays message "Token không h?p l? ho?c ?ã h?t h?n".
G.3 The User must restart the forgot password process.
G.4 Return to Step 1.
H. User remembers password (Step 1)
H.1 The User clicks "??ng nh?p" link.
H.2 The system redirects to the login page.
H.3 End Use Case.Exceptions:E. Unable to send OTP email (Step 5)
E.1 The system encounters an error when sending OTP email.
E.2 The system displays message "Không th? g?i email OTP. Vui lòng th? l?i sau".
E.3 End Use Case.
F. Rate limit exceeded (Step 5)
F.1 The User sends too many OTP requests in a short time.
F.2 The system displays message "Vui lòng ??i X giây tr??c khi g?i l?i mã OTP".
F.3 The User must wait for the cooldown period.
F.4 Return to Step 3.
G. Network connection lost
G.1 Network connection is interrupted.
G.2 The system displays network error message.
G.3 End Use Case.
H. Unknown system error
H.1 An unexpected system error occurs.
H.2 The system logs the error.
H.3 The system displays generic error message.
H.4 End Use Case.Priority:High, Must HaveFrequency of Use:Low to ModerateBusiness Rules:? Only accounts registered with email/password can reset password.
? OTP code is valid for 5 minutes.
? OTP code consists of 6 digits.
? Reset token is valid for 10 minutes.
? New password must be at least 6 characters.
? Must wait 60 seconds between OTP sends.Other Information:? Supports pasting OTP code from clipboard.
? Displays registered email on the reset password page.Assumptions:? The User has access to email to receive OTP.
? Email service is functioning normally.
UC06 Change Password

UC ID and Name:UC06 Change PasswordCreated By:DuyQKCE182413Date Created:26/01/2026Primary Actor:All usersSecondary Actors:NoneTrigger:The User wants to change current password to enhance security.Description:This Use Case allows the logged-in User to change their account password. The User needs to authenticate with the current password before setting a new password.Preconditions:? The User is logged into the system.
? The User has an account with provider = "local" (not Google/Social).
? The change password page is displayed.Postconditions:? The User's password is updated to the new password.
? The User can log in with the new password.
? The system displays success message.Normal Flow:1. The User accesses the change password page (from Profile or Settings page).
2. The User enters current password in the "M?t kh?u hi?n t?i" field.
3. The User enters new password in the "M?t kh?u m?i" field.
4. The User enters new password confirmation in the "Xác nh?n m?t kh?u" field.
5. The User clicks the "??i m?t kh?u" button.
6. The system validates the current password.
7. The system validates the new password.
8. The system updates the new password in the database.
9. The system displays message "??i m?t kh?u thành công".Alternative Flows:A. Current password is incorrect (Step 6)
A.1 The entered current password does not match.
A.2 The system displays message "M?t kh?u hi?n t?i không ?úng".
A.3 Return to Step 2.
B. New password not long enough (Step 7)
B.1 The new password is less than 6 characters.
B.2 The system displays message "M?t kh?u m?i ph?i có ít nh?t 6 ký t?".
B.3 Return to Step 3.
C. Password confirmation does not match (Step 7)
C.1 The password confirmation does not match the new password.
C.2 The system displays message "M?t kh?u m?i và xác nh?n m?t kh?u không kh?p".
C.3 Return to Step 4.
D. New password same as current password (Step 7)
D.1 The new password is the same as the current password.
D.2 The system displays message "M?t kh?u m?i ph?i khác v?i m?t kh?u hi?n t?i".
D.3 Return to Step 3.
E. Missing information (Step 5)
E.1 The User has not entered all required fields.
E.2 The system displays message "Vui lòng cung c?p ??y ?? m?t kh?u hi?n t?i, m?t kh?u m?i và xác nh?n m?t kh?u".
E.3 Return to Step 2.Exceptions:E. Account registered with Social Login (Step 1)
E.1 User logged in with Google/Social attempts to change password.
E.2 The system displays message "Tài kho?n này ??ng ký b?ng Google. Không th? ??i m?t kh?u".
E.3 End Use Case.
F. User does not exist (Step 6)
F.1 User not found in database.
F.2 The system displays message "Ng??i dùng không t?n t?i".
F.3 End Use Case.
G. Network connection lost (Step 5)
G.1 Network connection is interrupted.
G.2 The system displays network error message.
G.3 End Use Case.
H. Unauthorized - Token expired (Step 5)
H.1 Access token has expired.
H.2 The system returns 401 Unauthorized error.
H.3 The User is redirected to the login page.
H.4 End Use Case.
I. Unknown system error
I.1 An unexpected system error occurs.
I.2 The system logs the error.
I.3 The system displays generic error message.
I.4 End Use Case.Priority:Medium, Should HaveFrequency of Use:LowBusiness Rules:? Requires login (Bearer Token).
? Current password must be correct for authentication.
? New password must be at least 6 characters.
? New password must be different from current password.
? Password confirmation must match new password.
? Only accounts registered with email/password can change password.Other Information:? API endpoint: POST /api/auth/change-password
? Password is hashed before saving to database (using pre-save hook).Assumptions:? The User remembers current password.
? The User has stable internet connection.

UC08 View Food Categories 

UC ID and Name:UC08 View Food CategoryCreated By:TraLTCE180100Date Created:23/01/2026Primary Actor:CustomerSecondary Actors:NoneTrigger:The Customer selects a food category from the category list.Description:This Use Case allows the Customer to view a list of food items belonging to a selected category.Preconditions:? Food categories have been configured in the system.
? The food menu is accessible.Postconditions:? Food items filtered by the selected category are displayed.
? No data is modified in the system.Normal Flow:1. The Customer selects a food category.
2. The system requests food items associated with the selected category.
3. The system retrieves the filtered food list from the database.
4. The system displays the filtered food list to the Customer.Alternative Flows:A. No food items in category (Step 3)
A.1 The system finds no food items under the selected category.
A.2 The system displays the message “No food items available in this category.”
A.3 End Use Case.
B. Data loading failure (Step 2)
B.1 The system fails to retrieve food items by category.
B.2 The system displays an error message.
B.3 The Customer may retry.
B.4 Return to Step 2.Exceptions:E. Network connection lost (Step 2)
E.1 Network connection is interrupted during data retrieval.
E.2 The system displays a network error message.
E.3 End Use Case.
F. Invalid category (Step 1)
F.1 The selected category does not exist or is inactive.
F.2 The system displays the message “Invalid category.”
F.3 End Use Case.Priority:High, Must HaveFrequency of Use:FrequentBusiness Rules:? Only categories with status “Active” are selectable.
? Only food items with status “Active” are displayed.Other Information:Category selection may be implemented as tabs or filters.Assumptions:Categories are visible to all Customers without login.
UC09.1 View Canteens

3. The customer clicks the “Heart” button on a product.
4. The system verifies the customer’s login status and product validity.
5. The system checks whether the product already exists in the customer’s wishlist.
a. If the product is not in the wishlist ? proceed to Add Flow.
b. If the product is already in the wishlist ? proceed to Remove Flow.
Add Flow
6. The system adds the product to the customer’s wishlist.
7. The system updates the Heart icon to the “active” state.
8. The system displays a confirmation message
 (e.g., “Product added to Wishlist successfully.”).
Remove Flow
6. The system removes the product from the customer’s wishlist.
7. The system updates the Heart icon to the “inactive” state.
8. The system displays a confirmation message
 (e.g., “Product removed from Wishlist successfully.”).Alternative Flows:A.Customer Is Not Logged In (step 3)
     A.1 The system prompts the customer to log in or register before performing the action.

(e.g., “This product is unavailable.”) and cancels the action.Exceptions:E. System Fails to Add Product to Wishlist (step 5)
     E.1 A system error occurs while adding or removing the product.
     E.2 The system displays an error message
(e.g., “Unable to update wishlist. Please try again later.”).Priority:MediumFrequency of Use:FrequentlyBusiness Rules:? Only logged-in customers can add or remove products from their wishlist.
? A product can appear only once in the wishlist.
? Clicking the Heart button toggles the wishlist state (Add ? Remove).Other Information:? The wishlist should be accessible from the customer’s account dashboard and product pages.
? The system may limit the number of products in a wishlist (e.g., maximum 100 items).
? Wishlist actions may be logged for auditing or analytics purposes.Assumptions:None

UC12.1 View All Notifications

UC ID and Name:UC12.1 View All NotificationsCreated By:LocMTCE181795Date Created:14/02/2026Primary Actor:Customer, Staff, Canteen OwnerSecondary Actors:WebSocketTrigger:User clicks the Notification icon in the Header to open the Notification Dropdown or navigates to Notification page.Description:This Use Case allows Users to view all notifications belonging to their account. Notifications include order updates, promotions, system messages, feedback responses, shift updates, and salary updates (depending on role).Preconditions:? User has logged into the system.
? User has a valid JWT token.Postconditions:? Notification list is displayed with correct read/unread status.
? Unread count is updated correctly.Normal Flow:1. User clicks Notification icon.
2. Frontend sends GET request to API /api/notifications/my.
3. Backend verifies JWT token.
4. Backend retrieves notifications filtered by userId.
5. Backend returns notification list sorted by CreateAt (descending).
6. Frontend displays notifications including:
? Title
? Short Content preview
? Type
? CreateAt (format DD/MM/YYYY HH:mm)
? Read/Unread status
7. Frontend sends GET request to /api/notifications/unread-count.
8. Backend returns total unread notifications.
9. WebSocket listens for new notification event.
10. When new notification is pushed via WebSocket, frontend automatically inserts new notification at top and updates unread count.Alternative Flows:A. No Notifications
A.1 Backend returns empty array.
A.2 Frontend displays message “No notifications yet.”
A.3 End Use CaseExceptions:E. Unauthorized (Step 3)
E.1 JWT token is invalid or expired.
E.2 Backend returns 401 error.
E.3 Frontend redirects user to Login page.
E.4 End Use Case.
F. Network Error (Step 2)
F.1 Request fails due to network/server error.
F.2 Frontend displays message “Cannot load notifications.”
F.3 End Use Case.Priority:High, Must HaveFrequency of Use:Very FrequentBusiness Rules:? Users can only see notifications belonging to their own userId.
? Notification types depend on role.
? Notifications are sorted by newest first.Other Information:? Unread notifications are visually highlighted.
? Dropdown shows maximum 10 latest notifications.Assumptions:? User has stable internet connection.
? Notification data exists in database.
UC12.2 Mark as seen Notifications

UC ID and Name:UC12.2 Mark as Seen NotificationsCreated By:LocMTCE181795Date Created:14/02/2026Primary Actor:Staff, Canteen OwnerSecondary Actors:NoneTrigger:User clicks on a specific notification or clicks “Mark all as read”.Description:This Use Case allows Users to mark one or multiple notifications as read.Preconditions:? User is logged in.
? At least one unread notification exists.Postconditions:? Notification status is updated to IsRead = true.
? Unread count is decreased accordingly.Normal Flow:Normal Flow (Single Notification):
1. User clicks a notification item.
2. Frontend sends PATCH request to /api/notifications/:id/read.
3. Backend verifies JWT and ownership of notification.
4. Backend updates IsRead = true.
5. Backend returns success response.
6. Frontend updates UI to remove unread highlight.
7. Frontend refreshes unread count.
Normal Flow (Mark All):
1. User clicks “Mark all as read”.
2. Frontend sends PATCH request to /api/notifications/read-all.
3. Backend updates all notifications of user where IsRead = false.
4. Backend returns success response.
5. Frontend sets unread count to 0.Alternative Flows:A. Notification already read
A.1 Backend detects IsRead already true.
A.2 Backend returns success without update.
A.3 End Use Case.Exceptions:E. Notification not found
E.1 Backend cannot find notification by id.
E.2 Backend returns 404 error.
E.3 Frontend shows “Notification not found.”
F. Unauthorized
F.1 Backend returns 401 error.
F.2 Frontend redirects to Login.Priority:HighFrequency of Use:FrequentBusiness Rules:? Users can only mark their own notifications as read.Other Information:? UI update should be instant without full page reload.Assumptions:? Notification exists in database.
UC12.3 Filter Notification

UC ID and Name:UC12.3 Filter NotificationCreated By:LocMTCE181795Date Created:UC12.3 Filter NotificationPrimary Actor:Staff, Canteen OwnerSecondary Actors:NoneTrigger:User selects filter options (Type or Read Status) in Notification page.Description:This Use Case allows Users to filter notifications by type and read/unread status.Preconditions:? ? Notification list page is open.Postconditions:? Notification list is filtered according to selected criteria.Normal Flow:1. User selects Type filter (e.g., order, promotion, system, feedback, shift, salary).

3. Frontend sends GET request /api/notifications/my?type=shift&isRead=false.
4. Backend validates JWT.
5. Backend filters notifications by userId + type + isRead.
6. Backend returns filtered list.
7. Frontend updates notification list display.Alternative Flows:A. Clear Filter
A.1 User resets filters.
A.2 Frontend reloads full notification list.Exceptions:E. Invalid filter value
E.1 Backend detects invalid type.
E.2 Backend returns 400 error.
E.3 Frontend displays error message.Priority:MediumFrequency of Use:FrequentBusiness Rules:? Dashboard supports types: order, promotion, system, feedback, shift, salary.
? Client supports types: order, promotion, system, feedback.Other Information:? Filter is applied without page reload.Assumptions:? Filter is applied without page reload.

A.3 Return to Step 3.Exceptions:E. Rating not found (Step 1)
E.1 System cannot locate the rating.
E.2 System displays an error message.
E.3 End Use Case.
F. System error during update (Step 5)
F.1 Error occurs while saving changes.
F.2 System logs the error.
F.3 System displays an error message.
F.4 End Use Case.Priority:MediumFrequency of Use:LowBusiness Rules:Only the rating owner can edit the rating.Other Information:NoneAssumptions:Edited ratings may require re-approval.

C.2 Client displays notification "You have no orders yet".Exceptions:E1. Database Connection Error/Timeout (At step 6):

E.2 Client displays the message "System error, please try again later".Priority:HighFrequency of Use:HighBusiness Rules:? Students can only view their own orders 
? The latest orders are always displayed at the top (Sort DESC by createdAt).

? The old order belongs to the Student.
? The student is currently at the SAME Campus as the old order.Postconditions:?  The system creates a checkout draft with ONLY valid items (Active + In Today's Menu).
? Items not served today are removed, and a notification list is returned.
? Quantities are kept as original; if stock is insufficient, the item is removed.

4. System (Service): Fetches old order & verifies Campus match.
5. System (MenuSvc): Checks MenuSchedule to ensure items are served TODAY at THIS CAMPUS.
6. System (Service): Queries ProductModel to get current prices.
7. System (Logic): Filters valid items & recalculates total.
8. System (Controller): Returns 200 OK with valid items + removed items log.
9. Client: Redirects to Checkout Screen with pre-filled data.Alternative Flows:A. Different Campus Context (At step 4):
A.1 The system detects oldOrder.campusId != user.currentCampusId.

B.1 All items are removed due to Menu Schedule or Inactive status.
B.2 The system returns 400 Bad Requests.
B.3 Client shows "All items in this order are not served today".Exceptions:E. Database Error (At step 6):
E.1 System returns 500 Internal Server Error.
E.2 Client displays an error message.Priority:MediumFrequency of Use:MediumBusiness Rules:? Always use the CURRENT price from the Product table.
? Items must be present in the MenuSchedule for the current date and campus.
? Strict check on Campus ID equality.Other Information:Any previous Voucher applied to the old order is ignored.Assumptions:The database contains a valid Menu Schedule for the current day.

UC16.5 Payment Order

UC ID and Name:UC16.5_Payment OrderCreated By:ThaiTQCE181618Date Created:23/1/2025Primary Actor:CustomerSecondary Actors:MomoTrigger:Customer proceeds to payment and selects MoMo or Cash method.Description:This use case describes the process of a customer paying for an order using either MoMo online payment or Cash on pickup, and how the system records the payment status.Preconditions:? Customer must be logged in.
? Customer has items in their cart and can create an order.
? For MoMo: MoMo credentials are configured; backend payment endpoints are available.
? For Cash: Staff has access to update payment status after collection.Postconditions:? For MoMo: Payment status is updated to “completed” on success; “failed” otherwise.
? For Cash: Payment status is updated to “completed” when staff confirms receipt.Normal Flow:1. Customer opens the payment panel.
2. Customer selects a payment method (MoMo or Cash).
3. Customer confirms payment.
4. System creates the order with the selected payment method and sets payment status to “pending”.
5. If MoMo:
6. System creates a MoMo transaction and returns payUrl/deeplink to the frontend.
7. Customer completes payment in the MoMo app/web.
8. MoMo redirects back; the system verifies the signature and updates the order (completed or failed).
9. If Cash:
10. System keeps payment status as “pending”.
11. Customer pays at pickup; Staff later updates payment status to “completed”.
12. System displays the updated order/payment status to the customer.Alternative Flows:A. MoMo Payment Cancelled/Failed (step 6, 8)
     A.1 If the customer cancels or the gateway returns an error, the system sets payment status to “failed” and displays “Payment failed.”
     A.2 If redirect signature verification fails, the system sets payment status to “failed” and displays “Invalid payment response.”
B. Staff Unable to Confirm Cash (step 11)
     B.1 If staff cannot confirm cash payment, the order remains “pending.”
     B.2 Staff may mark “failed” due to discrepancy and the system displays “Payment failed.”
C. Order Not Found (step 8, 11)
     C.1 If the order cannot be found during update, the system displays “Order not found” and logs the error.
Exceptions:E. MoMo Endpoint/Network Error (step 5)
     E.1 If the payment request cannot be created, the system displays “Unable to initiate MoMo payment” and keeps status “pending.”
F. Signature Verification Error (step 8)
     F.2 If signature check fails, the system displays “Payment verification failed.”
G. System Error (any step)
     G.3 If the system fails, the system will display “System error, please try again later.”Priority:HighFrequency of Use:FrequentBusiness Rules:? Customers must be logged in to pay for orders.
? Payment method must be one of: cash, momo, vnpay, sepay, balance, bank_transfer.
? Payment status transitions: pending ? completed/failed/refunded.
? MoMo responses must be verified via HMAC signature.
? The UI should update in real-time to reflect payment status changes.Other Information:Observability: log payment outcomes and errors.Assumptions:The customer has a stable internet connection.
MoMo environment is properly configured (sandbox or production).

? The user is viewing the Cart or Checkout screen.
? The system knows the current Context Campus of the User.Postconditions:? No permanent voucher usage is recorded (This is just a validation step).

5. System (Service): Finds vouchers by code.
6. System (Logic): Executes Validation Chain: Date -> Campus -> Limit -> MinSpend.
7. System (Logic): Calculates discount amount based on Strategy (Percentage/Fixed).

C.1 The system returns 400 Bad Requests.
C.2 Client shows "Order must be at least [minAmount] to use this voucher".
D. Usage Limit Exceeded (At step 6):
D.1 The system returns 400 Bad Requests.
D.2 Client shows "Voucher has been fully redeemed".Exceptions:E. Database Error (At step 6):
E.1 System returns 500 Internal Server Error.
E.2 Client shows "System error, please try again later".Priority:HighFrequency of Use:MediumBusiness Rules:? Voucher must match the User's current Campus OR be a Global Voucher (campusId is null).
? Current date must be within startDate and endDate.
? If a percentage discount, the amount cannot exceed maxDiscount (if set).
? Final total cannot be negative (min 0).Other Information:N/AAssumptions:N/A

E.3 End Use Case.
F. Token expired (Step 3)
F.1 JWT token has expired or is blacklisted.
F.2 Backend returns 401 Unauthorized error.
F.3 Frontend handles redirect to login page.Priority:High, Must HaveFrequency of Use:Very frequentBusiness Rules:? EData is filtered by canteenId of the logged-in user, ensuring data isolation between canteens.
? GET API is public but controller reads canteenId from user (optional) to filter data.Other Information:? Frontend uses Ant Design Table component with rowKey as _id.
? "Actions" column displays Dropdown with 3 options: View, Edit, Delete.
? Updated date is formatted using dayjs in DD/MM/YYYY HH:mm format.Assumptions:? User has stable network connection.
? Backend service is operating normally.
UC17.2 View Ingredient Category Detail

UC ID and Name:UC17.2 View Ingredient Category DetailCreated By:DuyQKCE182413Date Created:02/02/2026Primary Actor:Staff, ManagerSecondary Actors:NoneTrigger:User clicks the "View" button in the Actions dropdown of an ingredient category row in the table.Description:This Use Case allows Users to view detailed information of a specific ingredient category. Information is displayed in a Modal including: category name, description, created date, updated date, and status.Preconditions:? User has logged into the Dashboard system.
? The ingredient categories list page is being displayed (UC17.1 completed).
? At least one ingredient category exists in the list.Postconditions:? The ingredient category detail Modal is displayed with complete information.Normal Flow:1. User clicks the "more_horiz" icon (?) on the row of data to view in the table.
2. System displays Dropdown menu with options: View, Edit, Delete.
3. User clicks the "View" option.
4. Frontend sends GET request to API /api/ingredient-categories/:id.
5. Backend finds ingredient category by id and canteenId.
6. Backend returns detailed information of the ingredient category.
7. Frontend opens Modal "Ingredient Category Detail" with information:
 - Category Name
 - Description (display "—" if empty)
 - Created At (format DD/MM/YYYY HH:mm)
 - Updated At (format DD/MM/YYYY HH:mm)
 - Status (Tag "Active")Alternative Flows:A. Close Modal (Step 7)
A.1 User clicks "X" button or clicks outside Modal area.
A.2 Modal is closed.
A.3 End Use Case.Exceptions:E. Ingredient category not found (Step 5)
E.1 Backend cannot find ingredient category with given id.
E.2 Backend returns 404 error "Ingredient category not found".
E.3 Frontend displays error message "Cannot load ingredient category details".
E.4 End Use Case.
F. Network error (Step 4)
F.1 Request fails due to network error or server error.
F.2 Frontend displays error message "Cannot load ingredient category details".
F.3 End Use Case.Priority:High, Must HaveFrequency of Use:FrequentBusiness Rules:? Can only view details of ingredient categories belonging to own canteen (backend filters by canteenId).
? Detail GET API is public but backend still checks canteenId to ensure data isolation.
? If description, createdAt or updatedAt fields are empty, display "—" character.Other Information:? Modal has width of 520px.
? Modal has no footer button (only X button to close).
? Information is displayed as read-only, no direct editing allowed on this Modal.Assumptions:? User has stable network connection.
? Ingredient category data exists in database.
UC17.3 Create Ingredient Category

UC ID and Name:UC17.3 Create Ingredient CategoryCreated By:DuyQKCE182413Date Created:02/02/2026Primary Actor:Staff, ManagerSecondary Actors:NoneTrigger:User clicks the "Create New" button on the ingredient categories list page.Description:This Use Case allows Users to create a new ingredient category for their canteen. User enters category name (required) and description (optional) into the form, system will validate data and create new ingredient category.Preconditions:? User has logged into the Dashboard system.
? User has role as admin, manager, or staff.
? User is assigned to a canteen (has canteenId).
? The ingredient categories list page is being displayed.Postconditions:? A new ingredient category is created in database with User's canteenId.
? isActive defaults to true.
? The ingredient categories list is reloaded to display the new record.Normal Flow:1. User clicks the "Create New" button at the top right corner of the page.
2. System sets formMode = "create", resets form and opens Modal "Create Ingredient Category".
3. User enters category name in "Category Name" field (required).
4. User enters description in "Description" field (optional, textarea).
5. User clicks "Create" button.
6. Frontend validates form (checks "Category Name" field is not empty).
7. Frontend sends POST request to API /api/ingredient-categories with body { name, description }.
8. Backend middleware protect authenticates JWT token.
9. Backend middleware restrictTo checks role (admin, manager, staff).
10. Backend controller gets canteenId from req.user and calls service.
11. Backend service checks category name is not duplicate (case-insensitive) within same canteen.
12. Backend creates new record in database with { canteenId, name, description }.
13. Backend returns 201 response with data of newly created category.
14. Frontend displays message "Ingredient category created".
15. Frontend closes Modal and calls fetchList() to update the list.Alternative Flows:A. Missing category name (Step 6)
A.1 User does not enter category name and clicks "Create".
A.2 Frontend displays validation error "Please enter category name" below "Category Name" field.
A.3 User re-enters information.
A.4 Return to Step 3.
B. Duplicate category name (Step 11)
B.1 Backend detects category name already exists in same canteen (case-insensitive check).
B.2 Backend returns 400 error with message about duplicate name.
B.3 Frontend displays error message from response.
B.4 User modifies category name and retries.
B.5 Return to Step 3.
C. Cancel creation (Step 3-4)
C.1 User clicks "Cancel" button or clicks outside Modal.
C.2 Modal is closed, form is reset.
C.3 End Use Case.Exceptions:E. User has no canteenId (Step 10)
E.1 User is not assigned to any canteen.
E.2 Backend returns 400 error "User does not belong to any canteen".
E.3 Frontend displays error message.
E.4 End Use Case.
F. No permission (Step 9)
F.1 User has inappropriate role (e.g., customer).
F.2 Backend returns 403 Forbidden error.
F.3 Frontend displays error message.
F.4 End Use Case.
G. Network or server error (Step 7)
G.1 Request fails due to network error or server error.
G.2 Frontend displays message "Cannot save data".
G.3 End Use Case.Priority:High, Must HaveFrequency of Use:ModerateBusiness Rules:? Category name is required, maximum 100 characters.
? Description is optional, maximum 500 characters.
? Category name must be unique within same canteen (case-insensitive check using regex).
? isActive defaults to true when creating.
? canteenId is taken from logged-in User information, cannot be passed from client.
? Only canteen owner, staff roles have permission to create.Other Information:? Form uses Ant Design Form component with "vertical" layout.
? Form has 2 fields: name (Input) and description (TextArea, 3 rows).
? requiredMark is hidden (requiredMark={false}).Assumptions:? User has stable network connection.
? User has been assigned to a valid canteen.
UC17.4 Update Ingredient Category

UC ID and Name:UC17.4 Update Ingredient CategoryCreated By:DuyQKCE182413Date Created:02/02/2026Primary Actor:Staff, ManagerSecondary Actors:NoneTrigger:User clicks the "Edit" option in the Actions dropdown of an ingredient category row in the table.Description:This Use Case allows Users to update information of an existing ingredient category. User can edit category name and description, system will validate data and update the record.Preconditions:? User has logged into the Dashboard system.
? User has role as admin, manager, or staff.
? The ingredient categories list page is being displayed (UC17.1 completed).
? The ingredient category to be edited exists in database.Postconditions:? Ingredient category information is updated in database.
? updatedAt field is automatically updated.
? The ingredient categories list is reloaded to display new information.Normal Flow:1. User clicks the "more_horiz" icon (?) on the row of data to edit in the table.
2. System displays Dropdown menu.
3. User clicks the "Edit" option.
4. System sets formMode = "edit", populates form with current data (name, description) and opens Modal "Update Ingredient Category".
5. User edits category name and/or description.
6. User clicks "Save" button.
7. Frontend validates form (checks "Category Name" field is not empty).
8. Frontend sends PATCH request to API /api/ingredient-categories/:id with body { name, description }.
9. Backend middleware protect authenticates JWT token.
10. Backend middleware restrictTo checks role (admin, manager, staff).
11. Backend service checks ingredient category exists by id.
12. If category name is changed, backend checks new name is not duplicate with other categories in same canteen (excluding itself).
13. Backend updates record with findByIdAndUpdate and option { new: true, runValidators: true }.
14. Backend returns 200 response with updated data.
15. Frontend displays message "Ingredient category updated".
16. Frontend closes Modal and calls fetchList() to update the list.Alternative Flows:A. Missing category name (Step 7)
A.1 User deletes all category name and clicks "Save".
A.2 Frontend displays validation error "Please enter category name".
A.3 User re-enters information.
A.4 Return to Step 5.
B. Duplicate category name (Step 12)
B.1 Backend detects new name already exists in another ingredient category in same canteen.
B.2 Backend returns 400 error with message about duplicate name.
B.3 Frontend displays error message from response.
B.4 User modifies category name and retries.
B.5 Return to Step 5.
C. Cancel update (Step 5)
C.1 User clicks "Cancel" button or clicks outside Modal.
C.2 Modal is closed.
C.3 End Use Case.Exceptions:E. Ingredient category does not exist (Step 11)
E.1 Backend cannot find record with given id.
E.2 Backend returns 404 error.
E.3 Frontend displays error message.
E.4 End Use Case.
F. No permission (Step 10)
F.1 User has inappropriate role.
F.2 Backend returns 403 Forbidden error.
F.3 Frontend displays error message.
F.4 End Use Case.
G. Network or server error (Step 8)
G.1 Request fails due to network error or server error.
G.2 Frontend displays message "Cannot save data".
G.3 End Use Case.Priority:High, Must HaveFrequency of Use:ModerateBusiness Rules:? Category name is required, maximum 100 characters.
? Description is optional, maximum 500 characters.
? When changing name, new name must be unique within same canteen (case-insensitive check, excluding current record being edited).
? Only admin, manager, staff roles have permission to update.
? Backend uses option runValidators: true to ensure validation during update.
? updatedAt field is automatically updated by Mongoose timestamps.Other Information:? Form is pre-populated with current data of ingredient category (name, description).
? Modal title changes to "Update Ingredient Category".
? OK button changes to "Save" instead of "Create".Assumptions:? User has stable network connection.
? Ingredient category record still exists in database when User submits form.
UC18.1 View Product Categories

UC ID and Name:UC18.1 View Product CategoriesCreated By:DuyQKCE182413Date Created:02/02/2026Primary Actor:Staff, ManagerSecondary Actors:NoneTrigger:User accesses the "Product Categories" page on Dashboard to view the list of product categories.Description:This Use Case allows Users to view a list of product categories belonging to their canteen. The list is displayed in a table with pagination, supporting sorting by name and created date, filtering by status.Preconditions:? User has logged into the Dashboard system.
? User has PRODUCT_READ or PRODUCT_CATEGORY_READ permission.
? User is assigned to a canteen (has canteenId).Postconditions:? The list of product categories is displayed in the table.
? Pagination information (current page, total records) is updated.Normal Flow:1. User accesses the "Product Categories" page on Dashboard.
2. System sends GET request to API /api/product-categories?page=1&limit=10.
3. Backend middleware protect authenticates JWT token.
4. Backend middleware requirePermission checks PRODUCT_READ or PRODUCT_CATEGORY_READ permission.
5. Backend controller gets canteenId from req.user and calls service.
6. Backend service queries the list of product categories by canteenId with pagination.
7. Backend returns the data list along with pagination information (page, limit, total).
8. Frontend displays data table with columns:
   - Category Name (supports A-Z sort)
   - Description (ellipsis when too long)
   - Status (Tag "Active" in green / "Inactive" in gray, supports filter)
   - Created Date (format DD/MM/YYYY HH:mm, supports sort)
   - Actions (Dropdown: View details, Edit, Delete)
9. Display pagination information "Total X categories" below the table.Alternative Flows:A. Change page (Step 9)
A.1 User clicks to change page or modifies records per page.
A.2 System sends GET request with new page and limit params.
A.3 Backend returns new page data.
A.4 Frontend updates table and pagination.
A.5 Return to Step 8.
B. Sort (Step 8)
B.1 User clicks "Category Name" or "Created Date" column header to sort.
B.2 Frontend sorts data on client-side (client-side sort).
B.3 Table updates display in new order.
C. Empty list (Step 7)
C.1 Backend returns empty data array.
C.2 Frontend displays empty table with default message.Exceptions:E. Error loading data (Step 2-7)
E.1 Request fails (network error, server error).
E.2 Frontend displays error message "Cannot load product categories list".
E.3 End Use Case.
F. No permission (Step 4)
F.1 User does not have PRODUCT_READ or PRODUCT_CATEGORY_READ permission.
F.2 Backend returns 403 Forbidden error.
F.3 Frontend displays error message.
F.4 End Use Case.
G. Token expired (Step 3)
G.1 JWT token has expired or is blacklisted.
G.2 Backend returns 401 Unauthorized error.
G.3 Frontend handles redirect to login page.Priority:High, Must HaveFrequency of Use:Very frequentBusiness Rules:? Data is filtered by canteenId of the logged-in user, ensuring data isolation between canteens.
? Default pagination: page = 1, limit = 10.
? User can change the number of records per page (showSizeChanger).
? All routes require authentication (protect) and permission check (requirePermission).
? Sort and filter by status column are performed on client-side (client-side).Other Information:? Frontend uses Ant Design Table component with rowKey as _id.
? "Status" column displays Tag with corresponding color: green (success) for "Active", gray (default) for "Inactive".
? "Description" column uses ellipsis attribute to truncate long content.
? Different from Ingredient Categories: Product Categories display actual isActive status from data, can sort and filter.Assumptions:? User has stable network connection.
? Backend service is operating normally.

UC18.3 Create Product Category

UC ID and Name:UC18.3 Create Product CategoryCreated By:DuyQKCE182413Date Created:02/02/2026Primary Actor:Staff, ManagerSecondary Actors:NoneTrigger:User clicks the "Add Category" button on the product categories list page.Description:This Use Case allows Users to create a new product category for their canteen.    - Category name is not empty

C.4 User modifies category name and retries.

D.2 Modal is closed, form is reset.

E.2 Frontend displays message "Please check the information again".
E.3 User corrects and retries.
E.4 Return to Step 4.Exceptions:E. User has no canteenId (Step 12)

E.2 Backend returns 400 error.
E.3 Frontend displays error message.

F.4 End Use Case.

G.2 Frontend displays error message "An error occurred" or message from response.
G.3 End Use Case.Priority:High, Must HaveFrequency of Use:ModerateBusiness Rules:? Category name is required, minimum 2 characters, maximum 100 characters.

? Category name must be unique within same canteen (case-insensitive check using regex).

? Modal has width of 600px.

UC18.4 Update Product Category

UC ID and Name:UC18.4 Update Product CategoryCreated By:DuyQKCE182413Date Created:02/02/2026Primary Actor:Staff, ManagerSecondary Actors:NoneTrigger:User clicks the "Edit" option in the Actions dropdown of a category, or clicks the "Edit" button on the detail Modal (UC18.2).Description:This Use Case allows Users to update information of an existing product category. User can edit category name, description and active status. System will validate data and update the record.Preconditions:? User has logged into the Dashboard system.

UC21.4 Handle Expired / No-show Orders

UC ID and Name:UC21.4 Handle Expired / No-show OrdersCreated By:HungNVCE182228Date Created:22/02/2026Primary Actor:System Timer (Cron Job)Secondary Actors:Staff, CustomerTrigger:The system's background scheduler triggers a sweep interval every 15 minutes.Description:Automatically scans and cancels ready orders that customers failed to pick up after the Canteen's operational hours have ended (specifically, 15 minutes past closing time). It then broadcasts real-time cancellation notices to both the Staff and the Customer.Preconditions:? The Cron Job scheduler must be actively running on the backend.
? There must be pending or "ready" orders belonging to active canteens.Postconditions:? Expired orders are permanently transitioned to the cancelled status.
? An automated cancellation reason is logged.
? Affected Staff and Customers receive immediate WebSocket notifications.Normal Flow:1. The system wakes up natively every 15 minutes based on the configured cron schedule.
2. The system queries active canteens and calculates their specific closing time baseline (closingTime + 15 minutes).
3. The system scans the database for any orders marked as "ready" belonging to canteens that have surpassed this time baseline.
4. For each expired order, the system updates its status to "cancelled" and appends the cancellation reason ("Auto-cancelled: Past closing hours").
5. The system saves the modified order records to the database.
6. The system dispatches real-time WebSocket notifications to the respective customer and the Canteen's dashboard informing them of the forced cancellation.Alternative Flows:A. Canteen Has Not Passed Closing Clearance (At Step 2):
A.1 The system compares the current time against the specific Canteen's operational hours (closingTime + 15 minutes).
A.2 The system detects that the current time is still within the acceptable pickup window.
A.3 The system bypasses this Canteen and immediately proceeds to evaluate the next active Canteen in the loop.
B. No Pending/Ready Orders Found (At Step 3):
B.1 The system determines that a Canteen has indeed surpassed the closing clearance limit.
B.2 The system queries the database but finds zero (0) orders belonging to this Canteen currently stuck in the "ready" status.
B.3 The system securely bypasses the cancellation payload logic (Steps 4-6) and proceeds to evaluate the next Canteen.Exceptions:E. Notification Delivery Failure (At Step 6):
E.1 The WebSocket channel fails to push the notification to the client.
E.2 The server logs the failed silent notification and proceeds to cancel the next order without crashing the loop.Priority:HighFrequency of Use:Continuous (Every 15 minutes)Business Rules:? Orders exceeding the Canteen's daily operational hours by 15 minutes without customer appearance are subject to immediate auto-cancellation to prevent carry-over data to the next day.Other Information:N/AAssumptions:The backend server environment natively supports and maintains uninterrupted Cron execution processes.

UC23.1 View All Foods

UC ID and Name:UC23.1 View All FoodCreated By:TraLTCE180100Date Created:24/0/2026Primary Actor:Manager, StaffSecondary Actors:NoneTrigger:Manager/Staff accesses the Product Management page or clicks the “Search/Refresh” button.Description:This use case allows Manager/Staff to view the complete list of food items belonging to their assigned canteen, with pagination, search, and filtering capabilities.Preconditions:? Manager/Staff has successfully logged into the system.
? User role is “manager” or “staff”.
? User has been assigned a valid canteenId.
? Network connection is stable.Postconditions:? The product list is displayed based on the user’s canteen.
? Pagination information is correctly updated.
? Data is cached for performance optimization.
? The view action is logged.Normal Flow:1. Manager/Staff navigates to the “Product Management” page.
2. The system verifies access permissions and retrieves the user’s canteenId.
3. The system calls API GET /api/products/manage with default parameters (page=1, limit=10, viewMode=active).
4. Backend filters products by canteenId and isDeleted=false.
5. Backend populates category and canteen information.
6. The system returns product list with pagination metadata (total, page, limit, totalPages).
7. Frontend displays products in table format with columns: Image, Name, Category, Price, Quantity, Status, Created Date, Actions.
8. Total number of products is shown in the table footer (e.g., “Total 25 products”).Alternative Flows:A. View Deleted Products (Step 3)
A.1 Manager/Staff switches to “Deleted” tab (viewMode=deleted).
A.2 System calls API GET /api/products/deleted.
A.3 Backend filters products with isDeleted=true and user’s canteenId.
A.4 Deleted product list is displayed with “Restore” button instead of “Delete”.
A.5 Return to Step 8.
B. Pagination (Step 8)
B.1 User clicks page number or changes items per page.
B.2 System calls API with updated page and limit parameters.
B.3 Backend calculates skip = (page - 1) * limit.
B.4 Returns products for selected page.
B.5 Return to Step 8.
C. Sorting (Step 7)
C.1 User clicks column header (Name, Price, Created Date).
C.2 Frontend toggles sort order (asc/desc).
C.3 Table re-renders sorted data.
C.4 Return to Step 8.Exceptions:E. Unauthorized Access (Step 2)
E.1 System detects missing canteenId or invalid role.
E.2 Backend returns 403 Forbidden with message: “You are not assigned to any canteen.”
E.3 Frontend displays error and redirects to Dashboard.
E.4 End Use Case.
F. API Connection Error (Step 6)
F.1 System fails to connect after 3 attempts.
F.2 Frontend displays: “Unable to load product list. Please try again.”
F.3 “Retry” button is shown.
F.4 If user clicks Retry, return to Step 3.
F.5 End Use Case.
G. Empty List (Step 7)
G.1 Backend returns empty array (total=0).
G.2 Frontend shows Empty State with message: “No products available.”
G.3 “Add New Product” button is displayed.
G.4 End Use Case.Priority:HighFrequency of Use:DailyBusiness Rules:? Staff/Manager can only view products belonging to their assigned canteen.
? Admin can view all products but cannot perform CRUD operations.
? By default, only non-deleted products are displayed (isDeleted=false).
? Products with recipe have stockQuantity = 0 and do not display stock.
? Default pagination is 10 items per page; options include 20, 50, 100.Other Information:API endpoint: GET /api/products/manage
Components: ProductManagement.jsx, ProductListView.jsx
Hook: useProductManagement.js
Service: product.service.js (getAllProducts)
Populate fields:
categoryId ? (name)
canteenId ? (name, location)Assumptions:? The user is authenticated and has a valid token stored in localStorage.
? The backend validates authorization using protect and restrictTo middleware.
? MongoDB has indexes on canteenId and isDeleted.
? Cloudinary thumbnails for product images are already available.
