? If a recipe exists, each ingredient is shown with quantity and unit.
? Inventory information is highlighted if low or out of stock.
? User can switch to edit mode from the detail modal (if allowed).Normal Flow:1. Manager/Staff clicks the “...” (more_vert) icon on the product row.
2. Dropdown displays the “View Detail” option (visibility icon).
3. Manager/Staff selects “View Detail”.
4. Frontend calls handleView(record) with productId.
5. Hook calls API:
 GET /api/products/:id with populate=categoryId,canteenId.
6. Backend validates that the product exists (including deleted products).
7. Backend populates:
a. Category (name)
b. Canteen (name, location)
8. Backend returns full product data with all fields.
9. Frontend sets selected = product and opens ProductDetailModal.
10. Modal displays a Collapse component with 8 sections:
Section 1: Basic Information
? Name, Canteen, Category, Slug, Description, Status
Section 2: Pricing & Sales
? Current price, Original price, Total sold, Rating
Section 3: Inventory
? Stock quantity, Low stock threshold, Warning indicator if low
Section 4: Recipe
? Ingredient table (if any)
Section 5: Images
? Main image + additional images
Section 6: Nutrition & Preparation
? Calories, Preparation time
Section 7: Special Status
? Popular flag, New product flag, Display order
Section 8: System Information
? Product ID, Created date, Updated date, Deleted date (if applicable)
11. By default, the first three sections are expanded (key=['1','2','3']).
12. Modal includes two buttons:
? “Close”
? “Edit” (primary type)Alternative Flows:A. Switch to Edit Mode (Step 12)
A.1 Manager/Staff clicks “Edit” in the detail modal.
A.2 System calls onClose() to close the detail modal.
A.3 Calls onEdit(product) to open ProductFormModal with mode="edit".
A.4 Data is pre-filled from the current product.
A.5 Transition to UC13 – Update Food.
B. View Full-Size Image (Step 10)
B.1 Manager/Staff clicks the main or additional image.
B.2 Image opens in a new browser tab (window.open(imageUrl, '_blank')).
B.3 Browser displays full-size image from Cloudinary.
B.4 Manager/Staff closes the image tab.
B.5 Return to detail modal.
C. View Recipe Details (Step 10, Section 4)
C.1 Manager/Staff expands the “Recipe” section.
C.2 If recipe.length > 0:
? Display table with columns: Ingredient Name, Quantity, Unit.
? Each row shows ingredientName, quantity, unit.
? The section header displays “X ingredients”.
C.3 If recipe.length = 0:
? Display Empty component with description “No recipe available.”
C.4 Return to Step 10.
D. View Deleted Product (Step 6)
D.1 Product has isDeleted = true.
D.2 Backend still returns the product (includeDeleted=true).
D.3 Section 8 displays deletion info:
? Light red background (#fff1f0).
? Delete icon with message:
 “Last deleted at: DD/MM/YYYY HH:mm”.
D.4 “Edit” button is disabled (cannot edit deleted product).
D.5 Return to Step 10.
E. Low Stock Warning (Step 10, Section 3)
E.1 stockQuantity > 0 and stockQuantity <= lowStockThreshold.
E.2 Display yellow warning box
E.3 Return to Step 10.
F. Out of Stock Warning (Step 10, Section 3)
F.1 stockQuantity = 0.
F.2 Display red error box
F.3 Return to Step 10.Exceptions:E. Product Not Found (Step 6)
E.1 Backend cannot find product with provided ID.
E.2 Return 404: “Product not found.”
E.3 Frontend displays: “Product no longer exists.”
E.4 Detail modal is not opened.
E.5 List refreshes.
E.6 End Use Case.
F. Error Loading Details (Step 5)
F.1 API timeout or server returns 500.
F.2 Frontend displays: “Unable to load product details.”
F.3 Modal is not opened.
F.4 User may retry by clicking “View Detail” again.
F.5 End Use Case.
G. No Permission to View (Step 6)
G.1 User is not staff/manager of that canteen.
G.2 Backend returns 403:
 “You do not have permission to access this product.”
G.3 Frontend displays an error message.
G.4 Modal is not opened.
G.5 End Use Case.
H. Missing Populated Data (Step 8)
H.1 categoryId or canteenId no longer exists (deleted).
H.2 Backend still returns product but category/canteen = null.
H.3 Frontend displays “N/A” for missing fields.
H.4 Modal displays remaining information normally.
H.5 Return to Step 10.Priority:MediumFrequency of Use:Daily (when reviewing detailed product information)Business Rules:? All authorized users (staff/manager/admin) can view product details.
? Staff/Manager can only view products of their own canteen.
? Admin can view all products.
? Deleted products can still be viewed but cannot be edited.
? Images are clickable to open full-size in a new tab.
? Dates are formatted in Vietnamese format (DD/MM/YYYY HH:mm).
? Null/undefined values are displayed as “Not updated yet”.
? Inventory is color-highlighted:
? Green (sufficient)
? Yellow (low stock)
? Red (out of stock)
? Recipe displays ingredient name from ingredientName or populated Ingredient model.
? Information presented must provide a comprehensive overview for Manager/Staff.Other Information:API endpoint: GET /api/products/:id
Component: ProductDetailModal.jsx
Hook: useProductManagement.js (handleViewDetail)
Service: product.service.js (getProductById)
Populate: categoryId (name), canteenId (name, location)
UI: Ant Design Collapse component with 8 panels
Image behavior: window.open(url, '_blank')Assumptions:? The product is fully populated with categories and canteens.
? Cloudinary URLs are valid and accessible.
? Recipe ingredients may be null or an empty array.
? Rating may be null (no reviews yet).
? Modal is centered with maxHeight = 90vh.
UC23.3 Create Food

UC ID and Name:UC23.3 Create FoodCreated By:TraLTCE180100Date Created:24/0/2026Primary Actor:Manager, StaffSecondary Actors:Cloudinary (Image Management)Trigger:Manager/Staff clicks the “Add Product” button on the Product Management page.Description:This use case allows Manager/Staff to create a new food item with complete information, including: name, category, price, images, description, status, stock quantity, or ingredient recipe.Preconditions:? Manager/Staff is logged in and has permission to perform product CRUD operations.
? User has a valid canteenId in the system.
? At least one product category exists in the canteen.
? Ingredient list is available (if creating a recipe-based product).Postconditions:? The new product is saved to the database with the user’s canteenId.
? Product images are uploaded to Cloudinary and their URLs are stored.
? A slug is automatically generated from the product name.
? Default status is set to “available” or “out_of_stock” (if stockQuantity = 0).
? The product list is refreshed and displays the new item.
? A success notification is shown to the user.Normal Flow:1. Manager/Staff clicks the “Add Product” button.
2. The system opens ProductFormModal with mode="create".
3. The form is initialized with default values (status=available, isNew=true, lowStockThreshold=10).
4. canteenId is automatically assigned from user.canteenId (not editable).
5. Manager/Staff enters required fields: product name, category, selling price.
6. Manager/Staff uploads images (maximum 6 images; the first image is the main image).
7. Manager/Staff chooses one of two stock management methods:
? Inventory Tab: Enter stockQuantity ? 0.
? Recipe Tab: Add ingredients with ingredientId, quantity, and unit.
8. Manager/Staff enters optional information: original price, description, calories, preparation time, isPopular.
9. Manager/Staff clicks “Create”.
10. The system validates data on the frontend (required fields, price ? 0, originalPrice ? price).
11. A FormData object is created containing all data (including image files).
12. Frontend calls API POST /api/products with Content-Type: multipart/form-data.
13. Backend validates permissions (checkUserCanCRUD, ensure user.canteenId exists).
14. Backend validates business rules (validateCreateProduct):
? Ensure product name is unique within the canteen.
? Ensure originalPrice ? price (if provided).
? Ensure consistency between recipe and stockQuantity.
? Ensure each ingredientId exists and is not duplicated.
15. Backend uploads images to Cloudinary (maximum 5 images).
16. A 300x300 thumbnail is generated for the main image.
17. Product is saved to MongoDB with Cloudinary publicIds.
18. Backend returns the newly created product (HTTP 201) with full information.
19. Frontend closes the modal, resets the form, and refreshes the product list.
20. Display notification: “Product created successfully.”Alternative Flows:A. Create Product with Recipe (Step 7)
A.1 Manager/Staff switches to the “Recipe” tab.
A.2 Clicks “Add Ingredient”.
A.3 Ingredient modal opens with ingredientId list (from API /api/ingredients).
A.4 After selecting ingredientId, the system auto-fills ingredientName.
A.5 Enter positive quantity and select unit (kg, gram, liter, ml, etc.).
A.6 Click “Add”; ingredient is added to the recipe table.
A.7 stockQuantity is automatically set to 0 and disabled.
A.8 Repeat A.2–A.6 for additional ingredients.
A.9 Return to Step 9.
B. Upload Multiple Images (Step 6)
B.1 Manager/Staff clicks “Upload Images”.
B.2 Selects multiple image files (maximum 6).
B.3 Frontend previews images as thumbnails.
B.4 The first image is marked as the main image (displayed larger).
B.5 Manager/Staff can remove or reorder images.
B.6 Return to Step 8.
C. Copy from Existing Product (Step 2)
C.1 Manager/Staff selects “Copy” from another product’s action menu.
C.2 The system opens the modal pre-filled with data from the original product.
C.3 Product name automatically adds prefix “Copy of ”.
C.4 Manager/Staff edits necessary information.
C.5 Return to Step 9.Exceptions:E. Frontend Validation Error (Step 10)
E.1 Required fields are missing (name, categoryId, price).
E.2 Red error message is displayed below the invalid field.
E.3 Focus moves to the first invalid field.
E.4 Notification: “Please check the information again.”
E.5 User corrects the data.
E.6 Return to Step 9.
F. Duplicate Product Name (Step 14)
F.1 Backend detects a product with the same name (case-insensitive) already exists in the canteen.
F.2 Returns HTTP 400 with message: “Product name already exists in this canteen.”
F.3 Frontend displays error notification.
F.4 User must change the product name.
F.5 Return to Step 5.
G. Cloudinary Upload Error (Step 15)
G.1 Cloudinary returns an error (network issue, quota exceeded, invalid file type).
G.2 Backend rolls back the transaction; product is not saved.
G.3 If partial uploads occurred, deleteMultipleImages is called.
G.4 Returns HTTP 500 with a detailed error message.
G.5 Frontend displays: “Unable to upload images. Please try again.”
G.6 User may retry or cancel.
G.7 End Use Case.
H. Recipe and Stock Consistency Error (Step 14)
H.1 Product has a recipe (length > 0) but stockQuantity > 0.
H.2 Backend returns HTTP 400: “Products with a recipe cannot have stockQuantity greater than 0.”
H.3 Frontend displays error notification.
H.4 User must either remove the recipe or set stockQuantity = 0.
H.5 Return to Step 7.
I. Ingredient Not Found (Step 14)
I.1 Backend checks and finds ingredientId does not exist in the database.
I.2 Returns HTTP 404: “Ingredients not found.”
I.3 Frontend displays error notification.
I.4 User must select a valid ingredient.
I.5 Return to Step 7 (A.2).Priority:HighFrequency of Use:Daily (whenever a new food item is added).Business Rules:? Product name must be unique within the same canteen (case-insensitive).
? canteenId is automatically assigned from the user and cannot be overridden.
? If a recipe exists ? stockQuantity must be 0 (managed via ingredients).
? If no recipe ? stockQuantity ? 0 is required.
? originalPrice ? price (if provided).
? Maximum 6 images; the first image is the main image.
? Slug is auto-generated from the name (lowercase, spaces replaced by “-”).
? Default status="available" unless stockQuantity=0 ? status="out_of_stock".
? isNew=true for all newly created products.
? Default lowStockThreshold = 10.Other Information:API endpoint: POST /api/products
Components: ProductFormModal.jsx, ProductForm.jsx
Service: product.service.js (createProduct)
Validation: product.validation.service.js (validateCreateProduct)
Upload handler: product.media.service.js (buildProductImagePayload)
Max file size: 5MB per image
Accepted formats: image/jpeg, image/png, image/webpAssumptions:? Cloudinary credentials are correctly configured in .env.
? Multer middleware is properly set up to handle multipart/form-data.
? User has sufficient permission to create products (role: staff/manager).
? Ingredient list is preloaded when the modal opens.

UC23.4 Update Food

UC ID and Name:UC23.4 Update FoodCreated By:TraLTCE180100Date Created:24/0/2026Primary Actor:Manager, StaffSecondary Actors:Cloudinary (Image Management)Trigger:Manager/Staff clicks the "Edit" button from the action menu of a product in the list.Description:This use case allows Manager/Staff to update information of an existing food item, including: changing price, updating images, editing recipe, adjusting inventory, and modifying status.Preconditions:? Manager/Staff is logged in and has product CRUD permission.
? The product to be edited exists and has not been deleted (isDeleted = false).
? The product belongs to the user's canteen (productCanteenId === user.canteenId).
? Category and ingredient lists are already available.Postconditions:? Product information is updated in the database.
? Old images (if replaced) are deleted from Cloudinary.
? New images are uploaded and their URLs are stored in the database.
? Slug is updated if the product name changes.
? Status is automatically synchronized if stockQuantity changes.
? updatedAt timestamp is updated.
? Product list is refreshed to display updated information.
? A success notification is shown to the user.Normal Flow:1. Manager/Staff clicks the "..." (more_vert) icon on the product row to edit.
2. A dropdown menu appears with the "Edit" option.
3. Manager/Staff selects "Edit".
4. System calls handleEdit(record) to open ProductFormModal with mode="edit".
5. The form is populated with the current product data:
? Basic information: name, categoryId, price, originalPrice, description, status
? Inventory: stockQuantity, lowStockThreshold
? Recipe: recipe (if any)
? Images: convert URLs into Upload component format (uid, name, status, url)
? CanteenId: displayed as disabled text (cannot be changed)
6. Manager/Staff edits necessary fields (name, price, images, description, etc.).
7. If images are changed:
? Upload new images (add to imageList)
? Remove old images (remove from imageList)
? Reorder images if needed
8. If recipe is changed:
? Add/remove/edit ingredients in the recipe table
? stockQuantity must be = 0 if recipe exists
9. Manager/Staff clicks the "Update" button.
10. System validates data on the frontend.
11. Create FormData containing modified fields and keepImageUrls (if keeping old images).
12. Frontend calls API:
 PATCH /api/products/:id
 Content-Type: multipart/form-data
13. Backend validates permission (checkProductAccess: product.canteenId === user.canteenId).
14. Backend validates business rules (validateUpdateProduct):
? Ensure name is not duplicated (excluding itself).
? Ensure originalPrice >= price.
? Ensure consistency between recipe and stockQuantity.
15. Image processing:
? If new images exist ? upload to Cloudinary.
? If keepImageUrls exist ? retain old images.
? Compare with current images to determine which to delete.
? Delete unused old images.
16. Update product in MongoDB using findByIdAndUpdate.
17. Automatically synchronize status:
? If stockQuantity = 0 ? status = "out_of_stock"
? If stockQuantity > 0 and status = "out_of_stock" ? validation error
18. Backend returns updated product with populated category and canteen.
19. Frontend closes modal, resets form, and refreshes product list.
20. Display success message:
 "Product updated successfully."Alternative Flows:A. Partial Update (Step 7)
A.1 Manager/Staff changes only 1–2 fields (e.g., price only).
A.2 Other fields remain unchanged.
A.3 FormData contains only modified fields + keepImageUrls (if images unchanged).
A.4 Backend updates only fields present in updateData.
A.5 Return to Step 19.
B. Remove All Images (Step 8)
B.1 Manager/Staff removes all images in the Upload component.
B.2 imageList = []
B.3 keepImageUrls = []
B.4 Backend deletes all old images from Cloudinary.
B.5 product.image = null, product.images = []
B.6 Return to Step 10.
C. Switch from stockQuantity to Recipe (Step 9)
C.1 Product initially uses stockQuantity (no recipe).
C.2 Manager/Staff adds at least one ingredient.
C.3 System automatically sets stockQuantity = 0 and disables the input.
C.4 Backend saves recipe and sets stockQuantity = 0.
C.5 Return to Step 19.
D. Switch from Recipe to stockQuantity (Step 9)
D.1 Product initially uses a recipe.
D.2 Manager/Staff removes all ingredients from the recipe.
D.3 recipe = []
D.4 System enables stockQuantity input.
D.5 Manager/Staff must enter stockQuantity >= 0.
D.6 Backend saves stockQuantity and removes recipe.
D.7 Return to Step 19.Exceptions:E. No Permission (Step 14)
E.1 Backend detects product.canteenId !== user.canteenId.
E.2 Return 403 error: "You do not have permission to modify this product."
E.3 Frontend displays an error message.
E.4 Modal closes automatically.
E.5 End Use Case.
F. Product Deleted (Step 14)
F.1 While editing, another user deletes the product.
F.2 Backend returns 404: "Product not found."
F.3 Frontend displays: "Product no longer exists."
F.4 Modal closes and list refreshes.
F.5 End Use Case.
G. Duplicate Product Name (Step 15)
G.1 Manager/Staff changes name to an existing product name in the same canteen.
G.2 Backend detects duplicates (excluding itself).
G.3 Return 400: "Product name already exists in the canteen."
G.4 Frontend displays an error message.
G.5 User must change the name.
G.6 Return to Step 7.
H. Error Deleting Old Images on Cloudinary (Step 16)
H.1 Cloudinary API fails to delete old images.
H.2 Backend logs error but does not roll back transaction.
H.3 Database is updated with new image URLs.
H.4 Background job handles failed deletions later.
H.5 Frontend receives a success response.
H.6 Return to Step 21.
I. Conflict Between Status and stockQuantity (Step 18)
I.1 User sets stockQuantity > 0 but status = "out_of_stock".
I.2 Backend validation error:
 "Stock quantity greater than 0 cannot have status out_of_stock."
I.3 Return 400 error.
I.4 Frontend displays an error message.
I.5 User must either adjust stock or change status.
I.6 Return to Step 7.Priority:HighFrequency of Use:Daily (price updates, inventory updates, status changes)Business Rules:? canteenId cannot be modified (security constraint).
? Only products belonging to the user's canteen can be edited.
? Updated product name must not duplicate another product in the same canteen.
? If recipe exists ? stockQuantity must be 0.
? If no recipe ? stockQuantity must be >= 0.
? originalPrice >= price (if provided).
? If stockQuantity = 0 ? status automatically becomes "out_of_stock".
? If stockQuantity > 0 ? status cannot be "out_of_stock".
? Old images are deleted only after successful upload of new images.
? updatedAt timestamp is automatically updated.Other Information:API endpoint: PATCH /api/products/:id
Component: ProductFormModal.jsx (mode="edit"), ProductForm.jsx
Service: product.service.js (updateProduct)
Validation: product.validation.service.js (validateUpdateProduct)
Upload: product.media.service.js (buildProductImagePayload, deleteProductImagesByUrls)
Image handling: Compare keepImageUrls with currentUrls to determine images to deleteAssumptions:? Product has not been deleted (isDeleted = false).
? The user has CRUD permission in that canteen.
? Cloudinary has a sufficient quota for new image uploads.
? Modal is mounted with fully populated product data.
UC23.5 Assign Food to Menu

UC ID and Name:UC23.5 Assign Food to MenuCreated By:TraLTCE180100Date Created:24/2/2026Primary Actor:Manager, StaffSecondary Actors:NoneTrigger:Manager/Staff clicks the “Assign Food to Menu” feature from the dashboard to manage menu items.Description:This use case allows Manager/Staff to view the full list of available food items in the canteen and assign selected items to a specific menu. The system supports selecting, previewing, ordering, and saving assigned foods into the chosen menu.Preconditions:? Manager/Staff is authenticated with a valid token.
? The user has a valid canteenId.
? At least one menu exists (draft or active).
? At least one available product exists in the canteen.Postconditions:Success:
? Selected foods are assigned to the chosen menu.
? Display order is stored correctly.
? Database menu document is updated.
? Success notification is displayed.
Failure:
? The menu remains unchanged.
? An appropriate error message is displayed.Normal Flow:1. Manager/Staff accesses the “Assign Food to Menu” page.
2. The system retrieves the list of menus belonging to the user’s canteen.
3. The system retrieves all available food items of the canteen.
4. The system displays a menu list (left panel) and food list (right panel).
5. Manager/Staff selects a menu to assign foods.
6. The system displays current assigned foods of the selected menu (if any).
7. Manager/Staff selects one or more food items from the available list.
8. The system adds selected items into the assigned list with automatic display order.
9. Manager/Staff clicks the “Assign” button.
10. The system validates selected menus and selected food items.
11. The system sends update requests to the backend.
12. Backend verifies authorization and updates menu items in the database.
13. Backend returns updated menu data.
14. Frontend refreshes menu state and displays success message.
15. Use case ends successfully.Alternative Flows:A. Remove Selected Food (Step 8)

A.2 System removes the item from the assigned list.
A.3 System recalculates display order.
A.4 System updates UI accordingly.

B. Preview Menu Before Saving (Step 9)

C. Duplicate Food Selection (Step 7)

E.4 User is redirected to login page.
E.5 End Use Case.

F. Menu Not Found (Step 12)
F.1 Backend cannot find the selected menu.
F.2 Backend returns HTTP 404 Not Found.
F.3 Frontend displays “Menu not found”.
F.4 End Use Case.

G. Validation Failure (Step 10)
G.1 No menu selected or no food selected.
G.2 System displays validation message.
G.3 API is not called.
G.4 Return to Step 9.Priority:HighFrequency of Use:Daily (menu updates and management activities)Business Rules:? Only foods with status “available” can be assigned.
? Staff/Manager can only assign foods within their own canteen.
? Each menu must contain at least one food item before activation.
? Display order is automatically generated based on selection sequence.
? A food item can belong to multiple menus.Other Information:API Endpoint: PATCH /api/menus/:menuId
Related Module: Menu Management
Backend verifies role before update.
Menu items stored as array { productId, order }.Assumptions:? Database and backend services are operational.
? Network connection is stable.
? The user interface clearly separates the menu list and food list.
? JWT middleware handles authentication before controller execution.UC23.6  Filter Food

UC ID and Name:UC23.6  Filter FoodCreated By:TraLTCE180100Date Created:24/0/2026Primary Actor:Manager, StaffSecondary Actors:NoneTrigger:Manager/Staff enters a keyword in the search box or selects a status filter.Description:This use case allows Manager/Staff to filter and search products in the list using multiple criteria: search by name, filter by status (available, unavailable, out_of_stock, hidden), filter by category, and sort by price/name/created date.Preconditions:? Manager/Staff is logged in and accesses the product management page.
? There is at least one product in the canteen.
? The product list has been successfully loaded (UC11).Postconditions:? The product list is filtered according to the selected criteria.
? Pagination resets to page 1.
? The number of matching results is displayed (e.g., “10 products”).
? Filter state is stored in URL query parameters (bookmarkable/shareable).
? If no results are found, an Empty state with suggestions is displayed.Normal Flow:1. Manager/Staff accesses the product management page (list already loaded).
2. Manager/Staff enters a keyword in the “Search products...” input (with search icon).
3. Manager/Staff presses Enter or clicks the “Search” button.
4. Frontend sets searchText state = entered value.
5. Frontend calls fetchList() with parameter search=searchText.
6. Backend receives query parameter search.
7. Backend builds MongoDB text search query:
 $text: { $search: searchText }
8. Backend searches in indexed fields: name, description.
9. Backend additionally filters by canteenId of the user and isDeleted=false.
10. Backend returns matching product list.
11. Frontend displays results with total count: “Total X products”.
12. If results exist ? display table normally.
13. If no results ? display Empty state: “No matching products found.”Alternative Flows:A. Filter by Status (Step 3)
A.1 Manager/Staff selects a status using Segmented Control.
A.2 Options: “All”, “Available”, “Unavailable”, “Out of stock”, “Hidden”.
A.3 Frontend sets filterStatus state = selected value (all, available, unavailable, out_of_stock, hidden).
A.4 Frontend calls fetchList() with status=filterStatus (if not “all”).
A.5 Backend filters products by status + canteenId + isDeleted=false.
A.6 Return to Step 11.
B. Combine Search + Status Filter (Step 3)
B.1 Manager/Staff enters the search keyword.
B.2 Manager/Staff selects a status (e.g., “Available”).
B.3 Frontend calls fetchList() with both search + status.
B.4 Backend applies both text search and status filter.
B.5 Backend returns products matching both conditions.
B.6 Return to Step 11.
C. Sort Results (Step 12)
C.1 Manager/Staff clicks a table column header (Name, Price, Created Date).
C.2 Frontend toggles sort order (asc/desc).
C.3 Frontend sorts current data (client-side) or recalls API.
C.4 Table displays sorted data with up/down arrow icon.
C.5 Return to Step 12.
D. Clear Filters (Step 3)
D.1 Manager/Staff clicks “X” (clear) icon in search input.
D.2 Or selects “All” in Segmented Control.
D.3 Frontend resets searchText = "" and filterStatus = "all".
D.4 Frontend calls fetchList() without search/status parameters.
D.5 Backend returns all products of the canteen.
D.6 Return to Step 11.
E. Filter Directly on Table (Step 12)
E.1 Manager/Staff clicks filter icon in “Status” column header.
E.2 Dropdown shows checkboxes for statuses.
E.3 Manager/Staff selects one or multiple statuses.
E.4 Table filters data client-side (onFilter).
E.5 Only matching rows are displayed.
E.6 Return to Step 12.
F. View Filter Guide (Step 2)
F.1 Manager/Staff clicks the “help_outline” icon.
F.2 Modal displays explanation of statuses:
? All: Show all products
? Available: Currently selling, customers can order
? Unavailable: Temporarily stopped selling, not visible to customers
? Out of stock: Stock quantity = 0, needs restocking
? Hidden: Under testing, not ready for sale
F.3 Modal includes a “Got it” button.
F.4 Manager/Staff closes modal.
F.5 Return to Step 3.Exceptions:E. Invalid Search Keyword (Step 7)
E.1 Search text contains special characters causing regex error.
E.2 Backend escapes the regex pattern to prevent injection.
E.3 Or returns an empty result if parsing fails.
E.4 Frontend displays Empty state: “No matching products found.”
E.5 End Use Case.
F. No Results Found (Step 10)
F.1 Backend returns empty array (data: [], total: 0).
F.2 Frontend displays Empty state with search_off icon.
F.3 Message:
 “No products found matching '{searchText}'.”
F.4 Suggestion:
 “Try another keyword or remove filters.”
F.5 “Clear Filters” button resets to full list.
F.6 End Use Case.
G. Error Loading Filtered Data (Step 6)
G.1 API returns 500 due to database issue.
G.2 Frontend displays:
 “Unable to load data. Please try again.”
G.3 Product list retains previous data (if available).
G.4 “Retry” button triggers fetchList() again.
G.5 End Use Case.Priority:HighFrequency of Use:Daily (whenever searching for specific products)Business Rules:? Search applies only to name and description (text index).
? Search is case-insensitive and supports Vietnamese diacritics.
? Status filter can be combined with search.
? Results are always filtered by the user’s canteenId.
? The default query only returns non-deleted products (isDeleted=false).
? Pagination resets to page=1 when applying new filters.
? Filter state is stored in URL query parameters (shareable/bookmarkable).
? Empty state displays friendly suggestions (not error messages).
? Sorting can be applied on filtered results.
? Filters are preserved when changing pages (pagination).Other Information:API endpoint:
 GET /api/products/manage?search=X&status=Y&page=1&limit=10
Component: ProductListView.jsx (filter controls)
Hook: useProductManagement.js (searchText, filterStatus state)
Service: product.service.js (getAllProducts)
MongoDB: Text index on name and description
Query helper: paginatedQuery with searchFields=['name', 'description']Assumptions:? MongoDB text index has been created on the Product model.
? Backend escapes regex to prevent injection attacks.
? Filter controls are clearly visible and easy to use.
? Empty state provides helpful guidance for users to refine search.

UC25.3 Create Schedule Menu

UC ID and Name:UC25.3_Create Schedule MenuCreated By:ThaiTQCE181618Date Created:24/2/2026Primary Actor:Staff, Canteen Owner, Admin SystemSecondary Actors:NoneTrigger:The canteen staff wants to configure a time range during which a menu is applied.Description:This Use Case allows Staff/Admin to create a new MenuSchedule that links a Menu to a Canteen within a [startAt, endAt] time range, with default status enabled.Preconditions:? The actor is logged in.
? The actor has role staff or admin.
? The corresponding Menu and Canteen exist.Postconditions:A new MenuSchedule record is created.Normal Flow:1. The actor opens the menu schedule configuration screen.
2. The actor selects the Menu and Canteen to link.
3. The actor selects the start time (startAt) and end time (endAt).
4. The actor clicks the “Save schedule” button.
5. The system receives a POST /api/menus/schedules request with scheduleData.
6. The system checks that startAt < endAt.
7. The system checks that the new time range does not overlap with any existing enabled schedule for the same Canteen.
8. The system creates a new MenuSchedule record.
9. The system returns the newly created schedule.
10. The UI displays the message “Menu schedule created successfully”.Alternative Flows:A. Invalid time range (Step 6)
   A.1 startAt >= endAt.
   A.2 The system returns an “Invalid time range” error.
   A.3 The actor adjusts the time range and retries (back to Step 3).
B. Overlapping schedule (Step 7)
   B.1 The system detects an existing enabled schedule overlapping with [startAt, endAt] for the same Canteen.
   B.2 The system returns an “Invalid time range” or overlap error.
   B.3 The actor adjusts the time range.Exceptions:E. Network connection lost (Step 5)
   E.1 Network connection is lost while sending the request.
   E.2 The system displays a network error message.
   E.3 End Use Case.
F. Unknown system error (Step 6–8)
   F.1 An unexpected error occurs while validating or saving the schedule.
   F.2 The system logs the error.
   F.3 The system returns a generic error message.
   F.4 End Use Case.Priority:HighFrequency of Use:Frequent (when configuring serving shifts/periods).Business Rules:? startAt must be earlier than endAt.
? No overlapping with other enabled schedules for the same Canteen.
? Default schedule status is enabled.Other Information:Schedules are the basis for determining the current menu of a Canteen.Assumptions:The actor has permission to configure schedules for the Canteen.
UC25.4 Duplicate Menu

UC ID and Name:UC25.4_Duplicate MenuCreated By:ThaiTQCE181618Date Created:24/2/2026Primary Actor:Staff, Canteen Owner, Admin SystemSecondary Actors:NoneTrigger:The user wants to duplicate an existing menu schedule to a new time range.Description:This Use Case allows Staff/Admin to create a new MenuSchedule based on an existing one (reusing menuId and canteenId) but with new startAt and endAt via the duplicate API.Preconditions:? The actor is logged in.
? The original schedule exists.Postconditions:A new MenuSchedule is created with the new time range, if valid.Normal Flow:1. The actor opens the schedule detail or list screen.
2. The actor selects the “Duplicate schedule” function.
3. The actor enters new startAt and endAt.
4. The actor confirms “Save new schedule”.
5. The system sends a POST /api/menus/schedules/:id/duplicate request with startAt and endAt.
6. The system validates the new time range (similar to createMenuSchedule).
7. The system finds the original schedule by id.
8. The system calls createMenuSchedule with the original menuId, canteenId and the new times.
9. The system returns the newly created schedule.
10. The UI displays the message “Schedule duplicated successfully”.Alternative Flows:A. Missing startAt or endAt (Step 5)
   A.1 The system detects one or both fields are missing.
   A.2 The system returns “startAt and endAt are required”.
   A.3 The actor re-enters complete information.
B. Original schedule does not exist (Step 7)
   B.1 The system cannot find the schedule by id.
   B.2 The system returns “Schedule not found”.
   B.3 End Use Case.
C. New time range invalid or overlapping (Step 6–8)
   C.1 startAt >= endAt or overlaps with an enabled schedule.
   C.2 The system throws an error from createMenuSchedule.
   C.3 The actor adjusts the time range.Exceptions:E. Network connection lost (Step 5)
   E.1 Network connection is lost while sending the request.
   E.2 The system displays a network error message.
   E.3 End Use Case.
F. Unknown system error (Step 7–8)
   F.1 An unexpected error occurs while duplicating.
   F.2 The system logs the error.
   F.3 The system returns a generic error message.
   F.4 End Use Case.Priority:MediumFrequency of Use:Moderate (for recurring/weekly schedules).Business Rules:? All rules from createMenuSchedule apply (valid time range, no overlap).
? menuId and canteenId are taken from the original schedule and cannot be changed via this operation.Other Information:Convenient for creating similar schedules across weeks or periods.Assumptions:The original schedule is valid and meaningful to reuse.
UC25.5 Toggle Scheduling Menu

UC ID and Name:UC25.5_Toggle Scheduling MenuCreated By:ThaiTQCE181618Date Created:24/2/2026Primary Actor:Staff, Canteen Owner, Admin SystemSecondary Actors:NoneTrigger:The user wants to enable or disable a menu schedule.Description:This Use Case allows Staff/Admin to toggle a MenuSchedule status between enabled and disabled with constraints: a running schedule cannot be disabled, an expired schedule cannot be enabled, and enabling must not cause overlaps with other enabled schedules.Preconditions:? The actor is logged in.
? The MenuSchedule exists.Postconditions:The schedule’s status is updated if valid.Normal Flow:1. The actor opens the schedule list.
2. The actor selects a schedule and clicks the toggle status button.
3. The system sends a PATCH /api/menus/schedules/:id/toggle request.
4. The system finds the schedule by id.
5. The system determines the current status (enabled/disabled).
6. If status is enabled:
7. The system checks whether the schedule is currently running.
8. If not running, the system changes status to disabled.
9. If status is disabled:
10. The system checks whether the schedule is expired (endAt < now).
11. The system checks that enabling this schedule does not overlap with other enabled schedules for the same Canteen.
12. If valid, the system changes status to enabled.
13. The system saves and returns the updated schedule.
14. The UI displays a “Schedule status updated successfully” message.Alternative Flows:A. Schedule does not exist (Step 4)
   A.1 The system returns “Schedule not found”.
   A.2 End Use Case.
B. Cannot disable a running schedule (Step 6)
   B.1 The system detects that now is within [startAt, endAt].
   B.2 The system returns “Cannot disable a running schedule”.
C. Cannot enable an expired schedule (Step 7)
   C.1 The system detects endAt < now.
   C.2 The system returns “Cannot enable expired schedule”.
D. Enabling causes overlap with other enabled schedule (Step 7)
   D.1 The system detects another enabled schedule overlapping with this schedule.
   D.2 The system returns “Schedule overlaps with existing active schedule”.Exceptions:E. Network connection lost (Step 3)
   E.1 Network connection is lost while sending the request.
   E.2 The system displays a network error message.
   E.3 End Use Case.
F. Unknown system error (Step 4–8)
   F.1 An unexpected error occurs during toggle processing.
   F.2 The system logs the error.
   F.3 The system returns a generic error message.
   F.4 End Use Case.Priority:HighFrequency of Use:ModerateBusiness Rules:? Running schedules cannot be disabled.
? Expired schedules cannot be enabled.
? When enabling, the schedule must not overlap with other enabled schedules for the same Canteen.Other Information:Provides a quick way to activate/deactivate menus by schedule.Assumptions:The actor understands the impact of enabling/disabling schedules.

UC26 Scan QR Code

UC ID and Name:UC_Order_06_Scan_QR_CodeCreated By:HungNVCE182228Date Created:22/02/2026Primary Actor:StaffSecondary Actors:NoneTrigger:The user clicks "Open Scanner" and focuses the camera on the customer's QR code.Description:Allows staff to use their device camera to scan a customer's unique Order QR code to instantly verify identity and complete the order handover process.Preconditions:? The user is logged in successfully.
? The user device has a functional camera with browser/app permission enabled.Postconditions:? The system automatically retrieves the order, marks it as completed, and notifies the staff.Normal Flow:1. The user navigates to the "Scan QR" screen.
2. The system activates the device camera.
3. The user points the camera at the customer's QR code.
4. The system decodes the QR payload locally.
5. The system sends the encrypted token/ID to the server for validation.
6. The server validates the token, confirms it is a pending order, and immediately updates its status to "Completed".
7. The system visualizes a "Scan Successful" animation and displays the briefly completed order snippet.Alternative Flows:A. Scan with Verification Step (At Step 6):
    A.1 Instead of auto-completing, the system is configured to require staff to physically check items.
    A.2 The system decodes the QR and shows the Order Details modal.
    A.3 The user physically hands over the items and clicks "Confirm Handover".
    A.4 The status is then updated to Completed.Exceptions:E. Camera Permission Denied (At Step 2):
    E.1 The device OS blocks camera access for the application.
    E.2 The system displays "Camera access is required. Please enable permissions or use Manual Entry."
F. Expired or Tampered QR Code (At Step 6):
    F.1 The server detects that the QR token is forged, expired, or belongs to a cancelled order.
    F.2 The system rejects the scan, playing an error sound, and displays "Invalid QR Code".Priority:HighFrequency of Use:HighBusiness Rules:? The QR token must have built-in security (e.g., JWT or hashing) to prevent customers from guessing order codes.
? A QR code can only be successfully scanned and processed once.Other Information:N/AAssumptions:The environment has adequate lighting for the camera to read the code.


UC26.1 Register Canteen

UC ID and Name:UC26.1_Register CanteenCreated By:ThaiTQDate Created:24/2/2026Primary Actor:Canteen OwnerSecondary Actors:NoneTrigger:The canteen owner wants to register a new canteen into the system.Description:This Use Case allows a canteen owner to create a new canteen profile in the system with basic information such as name, description, address, contact details, and operating hours. ? The canteen owner has a valid account and is logged in.

2. The actor fills in the canteen information (name, description, address, phone, email, operating hours, optional images, etc.).
3. The actor submits the “Register Canteen” request.
4. The system validates the input data (required fields, format of email/phone, length constraints, etc.).
5. The system verifies that the current user is allowed to register a canteen.
6. The system creates a new canteen record in the database, linked to the current user as owner.
7. The system sets the initial status for the canteen according to configuration (e.g., PENDING_APPROVAL).

   B.1 The system determines that the actor is not permitted to register a canteen (role or quota exceeded).
   B.2 The system returns an authorization/permission error.
   B.3 The UI displays an appropriate error message.Exceptions:E. Network connection lost (Step 3)
   E.1 Network connection is lost while sending the request.
   E.2 The system/UI displays a network error message.
   E.3 End Use Case.
F. Unknown system error (Step 6–7)
   F.1 An unexpected error occurs while creating the canteen.
   F.2 The system logs the error.
   F.3 The system returns a generic error message.
   F.4 End Use Case.Priority:HighFrequency of Use:OccasionalBusiness Rules:? A user must have the appropriate role (Canteen Owner) to register a canteen.

UC ID and Name:UC27.1_View FeedbackCreated By:ThaiTQCE181618Date Created:8/3/2026Primary Actor:Canteen OwnerSecondary Actors:NoneTrigger:The canteen manager wants to review customer feedback for dishes belonging to the canteen/campus they manage.Description:This Use Case allows a canteen manager to view a paginated list of feedback that customers have submitted for dishes in the manager’s canteen/campus. The manager can filter feedback by rating and by creation date, and can open a detail view of a specific feedback to see full information and any existing manager replies.Preconditions:? The system is running normally.
? The manager has a valid account, is logged in to the Dashboard, and is associated with a canteen/campus (has a canteenId).Postconditions:? The manager sees a list of feedback entries limited to dishes in their canteen/campus.
? The list reflects any filters applied (rating, from-date) and is paginated.Normal Flow:1. The actor navigates to the “Customer Feedback” screen in the Dashboard (Manager area).
2. The system automatically requests the feedback list from the backend, including the manager’s canteenId in the query.
3. The system returns a paginated list of feedback records that belong to dishes in the manager’s canteen/campus.
4. The UI displays the list in a table with key information per feedback:
a. Customer name
b. Product (dish) name
c. Rating (1–5 stars)
d. Comment (short text or placeholder if empty)
e. Created date/time
5. The actor may adjust filters on the list:
a. Select a specific rating or “All” (no rating filter).
b. Select a “From date” to see only feedback created on or after that date.
6. The UI sends a new request with the selected filters (rating, fromDate).
7. The system validates the filter parameters and maps fromDate to a createdAt ? fromDate condition.
8. The system applies the filters and returns the updated paginated feedback list.
9. The UI refreshes the table with the filtered results.
10. The actor can click “View / Reply” on a specific feedback row to open the feedback detail drawer.Alternative Flows:A. No feedback matches the current filters (Step 8–9)
   A.1 The system finds no records that satisfy the filter conditions.
   A.2 The UI displays an empty table or a “No data” message.
   A.3 The actor may adjust filters or clear them to broaden the search.
B. Actor changes page or page size (pagination)
   B.1 The actor navigates to another page or changes the page size in the table controls.
   B.2 The UI sends a new request with updated page and limit values and the current filters.
   B.3 The system returns the appropriate page of feedback data.
   B.4 The UI updates the table accordingly.Exceptions:E. Network or server error while loading feedback (Steps 2, 6, B.2)
   E.1 A network failure or backend error occurs during the request.
   E.2 The system/UI displays an error message (e.g., “Unable to load feedback list”).
   E.3 The actor can retry (e.g., using the “Refresh” button) or return later.
F. Unauthorized access (Step 2)
   F.1 The backend determines that the user is not properly authenticated or has no permission to access this module.
   F.2 The system returns an authorization error.
   F.3 The UI redirects the actor to the login screen or shows an appropriate error message.
   F.4 End Use Case.Priority:HighFrequency of Use:DailyBusiness Rules:? A manager can only see feedback associated with dishes in the canteen/campus linked to their account (via canteenId).
? Feedback data must be paginated to avoid overloading the UI and API.
? Filters must be applied server-side (rating and fromDate mapped to createdAt ? fromDate) to ensure consistency and performance.Other Information:? This Use Case is typically used by canteen managers for quality monitoring and service improvement.
? The list is integrated with a detail/reply Use Case for deeper handling of each feedback.Assumptions:? User authentication and authorization are already implemented and integrated with the Dashboard.
? Feedback data model is defined in the backend and supports canteen scoping, rating, comments, timestamps, and pagination.UC27.2 Reply Feedback

UC ID and Name:UC27.2_Reply FeedbackCreated By:ThaiTQCE181618Date Created:8/3/2026Primary Actor:Canteen OwnerSecondary Actors:NoneTrigger:The canteen manager wants to respond to a specific customer feedback to acknowledge the issue, provide an explanation, or inform about corrective actions.Description:This Use Case allows a canteen manager to open a specific feedback, view its full details and the existing reply history, and then add a new reply. The manager is also allowed to edit or delete replies that they previously created (or more broadly if they are an admin), while the original feedback content from the customer remains unchanged except where customer-side use cases apply.Preconditions:? UC26.1_View Feedback has been executed and the manager is on the “Customer Feedback” screen.
? The manager is logged in and has permission to view feedback for their canteen/campus.
? The targeted feedback still exists in the system.Postconditions:? A new reply may be created and associated with the selected feedback, or
? An existing reply may be updated or deleted, depending on the manager’s actions.
? The reply list for the feedback is refreshed to reflect the latest state.Normal Flow:1. From the feedback list, the actor clicks “View / Reply” for a specific feedback item.
2. The UI calls an API to load the full feedback details (customer, product, rating, comment, timestamps).
3. The UI also calls an API to load the list of existing replies associated with that feedback, ordered by creation time.
4. The system returns the feedback details and the reply list.
5. The UI opens a detail drawer showing:
a. Feedback section: customer name, dish name, rating, comment, timestamp.
b. “Manager Replies” section: chronological list of existing replies.
c. “Reply to Customer” form with a text area to enter a new reply message.
6. The actor types the reply content into the reply form.
7. The actor clicks the “Send Reply” button.
8. The UI validates that the reply content is not empty.
9. The UI calls the API to create a reply for the selected feedback, sending the reply text and the feedback ID.
10. The backend validates:
a. The feedback exists and is accessible.
b. The current user is authenticated and authorized to reply.
11. The backend creates a new reply record linked to the feedback and the manager’s user account.
12. The backend returns a success response with the created reply.
13. The UI shows a success message (e.g., “Reply sent to customer”) and refreshes the reply list for that feedback.
14. The updated reply list is displayed to the actor in the “Manager Replies” section.Alternative Flows:A. Edit an existing reply (owned by the manager)
   A.1 In the reply list, the actor sees a reply that belongs to the current user (or the user is an admin).
   A.2 The actor clicks the “Edit” button for that reply.
   A.3 The UI switches the reply into edit mode: the content is shown in a text area with “Save” and “Cancel” buttons.
   A.4 The actor modifies the text and clicks “Save”.
   A.5 The UI validates the content and sends an update request to the backend.
   A.6 The backend checks that the current user is allowed to update this reply (owner or admin).
   A.7 The backend updates the reply content and returns the updated reply.
   A.8 The UI shows a success message (e.g., “Reply updated”) and reloads the reply list.
B. Delete an existing reply (owned by the manager)
   B.1 In the reply list, the actor clicks the “Delete” button for a reply they own (or as an admin).
   B.2 The UI opens a confirmation dialog (Modal.confirm) asking the actor to confirm deletion.
   B.3 If the actor confirms, the UI sends a delete request for that reply to the backend.
   B.4 The backend validates that the user is authorized to delete this reply.
   B.5 The backend deletes the reply record.
   B.6 The UI shows a success message (e.g., “Reply deleted”) and refreshes the reply list.Exceptions:E. Feedback not found (Steps 2–3, 9–11)
   E.1 While loading details or creating a reply, the backend cannot find the specified feedback (e.g., it was deleted).
   E.2 The backend returns a “Feedback not found” error.
   E.3 The UI displays an appropriate error message and may close the detail drawer or disable further actions.
F. Unauthorized to reply or modify reply (Steps 10–11, A.6, B.4)
   F.1 The backend detects that the user is not allowed to create/edit/delete a reply for this feedback (e.g., not owner, not manager/admin).
   F.2 The backend returns an authorization error (e.g., HTTP 403).
   F.3 The UI notifies the actor that they do not have permission to perform the action.
G. Network or system error (Steps 2–3, 9, A.5, B.3)
   G.1 A network connection failure or unexpected server error occurs.
   G.2 The system logs the error (for system issues).
   G.3 The UI displays a generic error message and leaves existing data unchanged.
   G.4 End Use Case for that action (the actor may retry).Priority:HighFrequency of Use:DailyBusiness Rules:? Only authenticated managers/admins can reply to feedback.
? A reply is always associated with exactly one feedback and one user (the author).
? Only the reply’s owner or an admin can edit or delete a reply.
? Creating, editing, or deleting replies must not alter the original feedback content; feedback is modified only by feedback management rules.Other Information:? Replies are visible to customers in the client application as part of the feedback “conversation,” helping improve transparency and customer satisfaction.
? This UC is often used together with analytics (e.g., focusing on low-rating feedback first).Assumptions:? The feedback module is already implemented and provides stable IDs for feedback records.
? Authentication and role-based authorization are correctly enforced in the backend APIs used by this Use Case.
? The UI framework (Dashboard) supports modal dialogs and drawer-style detail views.UC28.1 View List of Staff

UC ID and Name:UC28.1 View List of StaffCreated By:LocMTCE181795Date Created:07/03/2026Primary Actor:Canteen OwnerSecondary Actors:NoneTrigger:Manager opens the Staff Management page from the dashboard sidebar.Description:This Use Case allows managers to view a paginated list of staff members belonging to their canteen. The list supports search and filtering options.Preconditions:? Manager is logged into the system.
? Manager account has role "manager".
? Manager account is associated with a valid canteenId.Postconditions:? A table containing staff members is displayed.
? Pagination information and staff details are shown.Normal Flow:1. Manager opens the Staff Management page.
2. Frontend sends GET /api/users/staff?page=1&limit=10.
3. Server verifies authentication and authorization.
4. Controller calls the service to retrieve staff list.
5. Service validates manager scope using canteenId.
6. Service queries staff users belonging to the canteen.
7. Pagination is applied to the query result.
8. Server returns staff list and pagination metadata.
9. Frontend renders the staff table including name, email, phone, gender, status, and actions.Alternative Flows:A. Filter or search (Step 2)
A.1 Manager enters keyword or selects filter values.
A.2 Frontend sends updated query parameters.
A.3 Server applies filters in the database query.
A.4 Filtered results are returned.
A.5 Frontend updates the table.
B. Change page (Step 9)
B.1 Manager selects another page in pagination.
B.2 Frontend sends request with updated page parameter.
B.3 Server returns corresponding page data.
B.4 Frontend updates table.Exceptions:E. Token invalid (Step 3)
E.1 Backend returns HTTP 401
E.2 Frontend redirects to login.
F. Unauthorized role (Step 3)
F.1 User role is not manager.
F.2 Server returns HTTP 403.
G. Network failure (Step 2)
G.1 Request fails due to network issue.
G.2 Frontend displays error message.Priority:High, Must HaveFrequency of Use:Very frequentBusiness Rules:? Staff data is filtered by the manager's canteenId.
? Only users with role "manager" can access this list.Other Information:? Frontend uses useStaffManagement hook to manage state and API calls.Assumptions:? Manager has stable internet connection.

G.2 Server returns HTTP 500.

H.2 Server returns HTTP 401 or 403.Priority:High, Must HaveFrequency of Use:ModerateBusiness Rules:? Only managers can create staff accounts.

UC29.1 View Staff Works Shift 

UC ID and Name:UC29.1 View Staff Works Shift Created By:LocMTCE181795Date Created:07/03/2026Primary Actor:Canteen OwnerSecondary Actors:NoneTrigger:Manager opens the Schedule Management page in the Dashboard.Description:This Use Case allows a Manager to view the weekly work schedule of staff in their canteen. The system displays shifts and assigned staff for each day of the selected week.Preconditions:? Manager has logged into the system.
? Manager account is associated with a canteen.
? At least one shift exists for the canteen.Postconditions:? Weekly schedule grid is displayed.
? The grid shows shifts as rows and days of the week as columns.
? Assigned staff appear in the corresponding cells.Normal Flow:1. Manager opens the Schedule Management page.
2. Frontend sends GET /api/shifts to load shift definitions of the canteen.
3. Backend returns active shifts filtered by canteenId.
4. Frontend sends GET /api/schedules/draft?weekStart=<date> to check if a draft schedule exists.
5. Backend returns draft schedule with its assignments if available.
6. If no draft exists, frontend sends GET /api/schedules/published?weekStart=<date>.
7. Backend returns the published schedule and its assignments.
8. Frontend renders the weekly schedule grid.
9. Each cell displays assigned staff for the corresponding shift and date.Alternative Flows:A. Switch week (Step 1)
A.1 Manager clicks previous week or next week navigation.
A.2 Frontend recalculates weekStart for the selected week.
A.3 Frontend reloads schedule data using the same APIs.
A.4 Continue to Step 2.
B. No draft schedule exists (Step 4)
B.1 Backend returns empty draft schedule.
B.2 Frontend requests published schedule instead.
B.3 Continue to Step 6.
C. No schedule exists for selected week (Step 7)
C.1 Backend returns empty schedule data.
C.2 Frontend renders schedule grid with no assignments.
C.3 End Use Case.Exceptions:E. Token invalid or expired (Step 2)
E.1 Backend authentication fails.
E.2 Backend returns HTTP 401 or 403.
E.3 Frontend shows error message.
E.4 End Use Case.
F. Network error (Step 2–7)
F.1 One of the API requests fails due to network error.
F.2 Frontend shows error toast message.
F.3 Loading state stops and grid is not updated.
F.4 End Use Case.Priority:High, Must HaveFrequency of Use:Very frequentBusiness Rules:? Schedule data is filtered by canteenId of the logged-in manager.
? Only one published schedule can exist for the same canteen and week.
? Draft schedule takes priority over published schedule if both exist.Other Information:? Schedule grid displays shifts as rows and days as columns.Assumptions:? Manager has stable internet connection.
UC29.2 Save Draft Schedule

UC ID and Name:UC29.2 Save Draft ScheduleCreated By:LocMTCE181795Date Created:07/03/2026Primary Actor:Canteen OwnerSecondary Actors:NoneTrigger:Manager clicks Save Draft on the schedule editor.Description:This Use Case allows the Manager to create or update a draft weekly schedule. Assignments are validated and stored together with the schedule using a MongoDB transaction.Preconditions:? User is logged in with valid JWT token.
? User role is manager or admin.
? Manager account must have canteenId.Postconditions:? A new Schedule record is created with status="draft".
? New StaffShift records are created with status="scheduled".
? The system returns saved assignments and ignored assignments.Normal Flow:1. Manager arranges staff shifts in the schedule grid.
2. Manager clicks Save Draft.
3. Frontend sends POST /api/schedules with { canteenId, weekStart, assignments }.
4. Server verifies authentication and authorization.
5. Controller calls scheduleService.createScheduleDraft(body, currentUser).
6. Service resolves canteenId based on user role.
7. Service validates assignment payload.
8. Service loads shifts belonging to the canteen.
9. Service validates each assignment.
10. Service starts MongoDB transaction.
11. System determines the next schedule version number.
12. System creates new Schedule record with status="draft".
13. System inserts StaffShift records for all assignments.
14. Transaction commits successfully.
15. Server returns { schedule, savedAssignments, ignoredAssignments }.Alternative Flows:A. Ignore past assignments (Step 9)
A.1 Assignment date is in the past.
A.2 System skips the assignment and increments ignoredAssignments.
A.3 Continue processing remaining assignments.
B. Ignore started shifts (Step 9)
B.1 Shift start time is earlier than current time.
B.2 System skips the assignment.
B.3 Continue processing remaining assignments.Exceptions:E. Empty assignment list (Step 7)
E.1 Assignment list is empty.
E.2 Server returns HTTP 400 "Assignment list cannot be empty".
F. Invalid shiftId (Step 9)
F.1 Shift does not exist.
F.2 Server returns HTTP 400.
G. Staff assigned multiple shifts at same time (Step 9)
G.1 Validation detects overlapping shifts.
G.2 Server returns HTTP 400 "Staff shift conflict".
H. Shift capacity exceeded (Step 9)
H.1 Assignment exceeds shift maxStaff.
H.2 Server returns HTTP 400.Priority:High, Must HaveFrequency of Use:WeeklyBusiness Rules:? Each schedule has a version number that increases with each draft save.
? Assignments must belong to shifts of the same canteen.
? Staff cannot work overlapping shifts on the same day.Other Information:? Draft schedule creation runs inside a MongoDB transaction to ensure atomicity.Assumptions:? Shift definitions already exist for the canteen.

UC29.4 Publish Schedule

UC ID and Name:UC29.4 Publish ScheduleCreated By:LocMTCE181795Date Created:07/03/2026Primary Actor:Canteen OwnerSecondary Actors:NoneTrigger:Manager clicks Publish Schedule on the schedule editor.Description:This Use Case allows a manager to officially publish a draft schedule. The schedule status changes to "published", previous published schedules for the same week are archived, and assigned staff receive notifications.Preconditions:? User is logged in with a valid JWT token.
? User role is manager or admin.
? The schedule exists and has status "draft".
? The schedule contains at least one valid assignment.Postconditions:? Schedule status becomes "published".
? publishedAt timestamp is set.
? Previous published schedules of the same week are archived.
? Notifications are created for assigned staff.
? Staff receive real-time notification if connected via WebSocket.Normal Flow:1. Manager reviews the draft schedule.
2. Manager clicks Publish Schedule.
3. Frontend displays confirmation dialog.
4. Manager confirms publishing action.
5. Frontend sends POST /api/schedules/:id/publish with Bearer Token.
6. Server verifies authentication and authorization.
7. Controller calls scheduleService.publishSchedule(scheduleId, currentUser).
8. Service finds the schedule by ID.
9. Service verifies the schedule belongs to the correct canteen.
10. Service checks that schedule status is "draft".
11. MongoDB transaction begins.
12. Service verifies that schedule has assignments.
13. System archives previous published schedules of the same canteen and week.
14. System updates the selected schedule status to "published" and sets publishedAt.
15. Service collects all distinct staffId values from assignments.
16. Notification records are created for each staff.
17. WebSocket notifications are sent to connected staff.
18. Transaction commits.
19. Server returns the published schedule.
20. Frontend displays success notification.Alternative Flows:A. Manager cancels publish confirmation (Step 3)
A.1 Manager closes the confirmation dialog.
A.2 Publishing process is cancelled.
A.3 Draft schedule remains unchanged.
B. Previous schedule exists (Step 13)
B.1 System detects previous published schedule for the same week.
B.2 System archives previous schedule automatically.
B.3 Continue to Step 14.Exceptions:E. Schedule not found (Step 8)
E.1 Schedule ID does not exist.
E.2 Server returns HTTP 404 "Schedule not found".
F. Unauthorized canteen access (Step 9)
F.1 Schedule belongs to another canteen.
F.2 Server returns HTTP 403.
G. Invalid schedule status (Step 10)
G.1 Schedule status is not "draft".
G.2 Server returns HTTP 400.
H. Empty draft schedule (Step 12)
H.1 Draft schedule contains no assignments.
H.2 Server returns HTTP 400 "Cannot publish empty schedule".Priority:High, Must HaveFrequency of Use:WeeklyBusiness Rules:? Only draft schedules can be published.
? Only one published schedule can exist per canteen and week.
? Publishing triggers staff notifications.Other Information:? Notification includes schedule information and assigned shift details.Assumptions:? Staff devices are connected to receive real-time notifications.

UC30.2 Filter/Search Payroll

? System clock is accurate for default year.UC30.3 Calculate Payroll (Generate Payslip)

UC ID and Name:UC30.3 Calculate Payroll (Generate Payslip)Created By:DuyQKCE182413Date Created:07/03/2026Primary Actor:Canteen OwnerSecondary Actors:NoneTrigger:User clicks the "T?o b?ng l??ng m?i" button on Payroll List page.Description:This Use Case allows Users to automatically generate a payroll period and calculate salaries for all staff members who worked during the selected period. The system queries attendance data, applies salary rate configurations, calculates bonuses and deductions, and creates salary records.Preconditions:? User has logged into the Dashboard system.
? User has role as admin, canteen_owner, or manager.
? The Payroll List page is being displayed.
? Staff members have checked-in/checked-out shifts during the selected period.
? Salary rate configurations exist for staff (optional, falls back to default hourly rate).Postconditions:? A new Payroll record is created with status "calculated".
? Salary records are created for all staff members with attendance.
? Bonuses and deductions are automatically calculated based on attendance patterns.
? Total amount, bonus, and deduction are aggregated in Payroll.
? The Payroll List is refreshed to show the new period.Normal Flow:1. User clicks "T?o b?ng l??ng m?i" button.
2. System opens GeneratePayrollDialog modal.
3. User enters required information:
   - T? ngày (periodStart): date picker
   - ??n ngày (periodEnd): date picker
   - M?c l??ng gi? m?c ??nh (hourlyRate): number input (VND)
   - Mô t? (description): text area (optional)
4. User clicks "T?o b?ng l??ng" button.
5. Frontend validates:
   - All required fields are filled
   - periodEnd > periodStart
   - hourlyRate > 0
6. Frontend sends POST request to /api/payrolls/generate with body:
json<br>{<br> "canteenId": "...",<br> "periodStart": "2026-01-01",<br> "periodEnd": "2026-01-31",<br> "hourlyRate": 50000,<br> "description": "..."<br>}<br>
7. Backend generatePayroll service starts transaction:
   a. Check for duplicate payroll (same canteen + period)
   b. Create Payroll record with status "draft"
   c. Query all StaffShift with status "checked_out" in period
   d. Aggregate total hours by userId
   e. Fetch all SalaryRate configs for canteen
   f. For each staff:
    i. Get hourly rate (from SalaryRate or fallback to default)
    ii. Calculate baseSalary = totalHours × hourlyRate
    iii. Call calculateAdjustments(userId, canteenId, periodStart, periodEnd) to get bonus/deduction
    iv. Create Salary record with all calculations
   g. Update Payroll with aggregated totals (totalStaff, totalHours, totalAmount, totalBonus, totalDeduction)
   h. Set Payroll status to "calculated"
   i. Commit transaction
8. Backend returns 201 response with message "?ã t?o k? l??ng cho {n} nhân viên".
9. Frontend displays success message.
10. Frontend closes modal and refreshes payroll list.Alternative Flows:A. No staff with attendance (Step 7c)
A.1 StaffShift query returns empty array.
A.2 Backend rolls back transaction.
A.3 Backend returns 400 error "Không có nhân viên nào làm vi?c trong k? này".
A.4 Frontend displays error message.
A.5 End Use Case.
B. Duplicate payroll period (Step 7a)
B.1 Backend finds existing payroll with same canteenId + period.
B.2 Backend returns 400 error "K? l??ng này ?ã t?n t?i cho c?ng tin này".
B.3 Frontend displays error message.
B.4 User modifies period dates.
B.5 Return to Step 3.
C. Cancel creation (Step 3-4)
C.1 User clicks "H?y" button or clicks outside modal.
C.2 Modal is closed, form is reset.
C.3 End Use Case.Exceptions:E. Invalid date range (Step 5)
E.1 periodEnd <= periodStart.
E.2 Frontend displays validation error "Ngày k?t thúc ph?i sau ngày b?t ??u".
E.3 Return to Step 3.
F. Invalid hourly rate (Step 5)
F.1 hourlyRate <= 0 or not a number.
F.2 Frontend displays validation error "Vui lòng nh?p m?c l??ng gi? h?p l?".
F.3 Return to Step 3.
G. Transaction failure (Step 7)
G.1 Database operation fails during transaction.
G.2 Backend rolls back all changes.
G.3 Backend returns 500 error.
G.4 Frontend displays error message "L?i khi t?o b?ng l??ng".
G.5 End Use Case.Priority:Critical, Must HaveFrequency of Use:Monthly (or per payroll cycle)Business Rules:? Only checked-out shifts are counted for salary calculation.
? Individual salary rate overrides the default hourly rate.
? Attendance bonuses are applied based on attendance rate (100%, ?95%, ?90%).
? Overtime pay is calculated based on overtime hours and the overtime multiplier.
? Late arrivals, early leaves, and absences may result in salary deductions.
? If late arrivals exceed the allowed limit, attendance bonus is removed.
? Payroll periods must not overlap.
? Payroll status progresses from Draft ? Calculated after payroll generation.Other Information:? A loading state is displayed during payroll generation.
? Payroll calculations are processed by a dedicated calculation module.
? Calculation details are stored for auditing and traceability.Assumptions:? • Attendance data (StaffShift) is accurate and complete.
? • SalaryRate configurations are up-to-date.
? • System has sufficient performance to handle concurrent calculations.UC30.4 View salary rate

UC ID and Name:UC30.4 View Salary Rate ConfigurationCreated By:DuyQKCE182413Date Created:07/03/2026Primary Actor:Canteen OwnerSecondary Actors:NoneTrigger:User accesses the "Qu?n lý l??ng" > "C?u hình l??ng" page on Dashboard.Description:This Use Case allows Users to view salary rate configurations for all staff members in their canteen. Each configuration includes hourly rate, attendance bonuses, overtime multiplier, deduction penalties, and other settings that determine how salary is calculated.Preconditions:? User has logged into the Dashboard system.
? User has role as admin, canteen_owner, or manager.

   - T?ng nhân viên ?ã c?u hình
   - L??ng gi? TB (average)
   - L??ng gi? cao nh?t (max, green)
   - L??ng gi? th?p nh?t (min, orange)

   - Nhân viên (name + email)
   - L??ng gi? (VND, blue tag)
   - Th??ng chuyên c?n (3 levels: 100%, ?95%, ?90%)
   - H? s? OT (multiplier, green tag)
   - Ph?t (late/early/absent in VND)
   - Cho phép mu?n (max late allowed)
   - Thao tác (Edit button)Alternative Flows:A. Empty list (Step 6)

   - M?c l??ng theo gi? (required, VND)
   - Th??ng 100%/?95%/?90% ca (3 fields, VND)
   - H? s? th??ng overtime (decimal, 1-5)
   - Ph?t ?i mu?n/v? s?m/ngh? KP (3 fields, VND)

   - Ghi chú (optional, textarea)

For Update:

3. System pre-fills form with existing values.
4. User modifies desired values (userId field is hidden, not editable).
5. Continue from step 6 above.

9. Backend validates request data.

A.4 End Use Case.

B.2 Modal closes without saving.
B.3 Form is reset.
B.4 End Use Case.Exceptions:E. Validation errors (Step 7)

E.2 Frontend displays red validation message below field.

E.4 User corrects input.
E.5 Return to Step 6.

F.3 Continue to Step 9.

G.2 Database unique constraint violation occurs.
G.3 Backend returns 400 error.
G.4 Frontend displays error message.
G.5 End Use Case.
H. Server error (Step 10-11)
H.1 Database operation fails.
H.2 Backend returns 500 error.
H.3 Frontend displays message "L?i khi l?u c?u hình l??ng".
H.4 End Use Case.Priority:High, Must HaveFrequency of Use:Occasional (when onboarding new staff or adjusting rates)Business Rules:? Each user can have only one salary rate configuration.

UC30.7 Adjust Payroll

UC ID and Name:UC30.7 Adjust Payroll (Salary)Created By:DuyQKCE182413Date Created:07/03/2026Primary Actor:Canteen OwnerSecondary Actors:NoneTrigger:User clicks "?i?u ch?nh" button on a salary record in the Payroll Detail page.Description:This Use Case allows Users to manually adjust a staff member's salary record within a payroll period. User has logged into the Dashboard system.

? totalSalary is recalculated automatically.

   - Nhân viên: staff fullName (read-only)
   - L??ng c? b?n: baseSalary (read-only, VND)
   - Th??ng hi?n t?i: current bonus (editable, VND)
   - Kh?u tr? hi?n t?i: current deduction (editable, VND)
   - T?ng l??ng: calculated totalSalary (read-only, auto-updates)

10. Frontend validates inputs (bonus >= 0, deduction >= 0, reason not empty).
11. Frontend sends PATCH to /api/payrolls/:payrollId/salaries/:salaryId with body:
json<br>{<br> "bonus": 200000,<br> "deduction": 50000,<br> "adjustmentReason": "Th??ng hoàn thành xu?t s?c"<br>}<br>
12. Backend validates payroll status (must not be locked).
13. Backend updates Salary record using service method.
14. Backend recalculates totalSalary = baseSalary + bonus - deduction.
15. Backend updates Payroll aggregate totals.
16. Backend returns updated salary record.
17. Frontend displays success message "?ã ?i?u ch?nh l??ng thành công".
18. Frontend closes modal and refreshes payroll detail.Alternative Flows:A. Cancel adjustment (Step 9)
A.1 User clicks "H?y" button or clicks outside modal.
A.2 Modal closes without saving changes.
A.3 End Use Case.

B.1 adjustmentReason field is empty or null.
B.2 Display "—" or "Không có lý do chi ti?t".
B.3 Continue normal flow.Exceptions:E. Payroll is locked (Step 12)
E.1 Payroll status is "approved" or "paid" or isLocked=true.
E.2 Backend returns 400 error "Không th? ?i?u ch?nh k? l??ng ?ã khóa".
E.3 Frontend displays an error message.
E.4 End Use Case.
F. Invalid values (Step 10)
F.1 Bonus or deduction is negative.
F.2 Frontend displays validation error "Giá tr? không ???c âm".
F.3 User corrects input.
F.4 Return to Step 9.
G. Missing adjustment reason (Step 10)
G.1 adjustmentReason field is empty.
G.2 Frontend displays validation error "Vui lòng nh?p lý do ?i?u ch?nh".
G.3 User enters reason.
G.4 Return to Step 9.
H. Server error (Step 13-15)
H.1 Database update fails.
H.2 Backend returns 500 error.
H.3 Frontend displays message "L?i khi ?i?u ch?nh l??ng".
H.4 End Use Case.Priority:High, Must HaveFrequency of Use:Occasional (as needed for corrections or special cases)Business Rules:? Salaries can only be adjusted when the payroll status is Draft or Calculated.
? Approved or Paid payrolls cannot be modified.

? Original calculated salary values are preserved for history and auditing.Other Information:? Modal uses Ant Design Form with vertical layout.
? Currency formatters are applied to numeric inputs.

UC ID and Name:UC30.8 Approve PayrollCreated By:DuyQKCE182413Date Created:07/03/2026Primary Actor:Canteen OwnerSecondary Actors:NoneTrigger:User clicks "Duy?t k? l??ng" button on a payroll with "calculated" status.Description:This Use Case allows Users to approve a calculated payroll period.
