User has logged into the Dashboard system.
? User has role as admin, canteen_owner, or manager.
? The payroll exists with status "calculated".
? The payroll belongs to user's canteen.
? All salary records in the payroll have been reviewed.Postconditions:? Payroll status is changed from "calculated" to "approved".
? Payroll isLocked is set to true (no further modifications allowed).
? approvedBy field is set to current user._id.
? approvedAt timestamp is recorded.
? All salary records in the payroll are marked as "approved".
? The payroll is ready for payment processing.Normal Flow:1. User is viewing either Payroll List or Payroll Detail page.
2. User identifies payroll with "calculated" status (blue tag).
3. User clicks "Duy?t k? l??ng" button (CheckCircleOutlined icon).
4. System displays Popconfirm dialog: "B?n có ch?c mu?n duy?t k? l??ng này? Sau khi duy?t s? không th? ch?nh s?a."
5. User clicks "??ng ý" button.
6. Frontend sends PATCH request to /api/payrolls/:id/approve.
7. Backend validates:
   a. Payroll exists
   b. Payroll belongs to user's canteen
   c. Current status is "calculated"
   d. Payroll is not locked
8. Backend updates Payroll:
   - status = "approved"
   - isLocked = true
   - approvedBy = req.user._id
   - approvedAt = new Date()
9. Backend updates all related Salary records:
   - status = "approved"
10. Backend returns updated payroll.
11. Frontend displays success message "?ã duy?t k? l??ng".
12. Frontend refreshes the view (list or detail).Alternative Flows:A. Cancel approval (Step 5)
A.1 User clicks "H?y" in Popconfirm dialog.
A.2 Dialog closes without making changes.
A.3 End Use Case.Exceptions:E. Invalid status (Step 7c)
E.1 Payroll status is not "calculated" (e.g., already approved, or still draft).
E.2 Backend returns 400 error "Không th? duy?t k? l??ng v?i tr?ng thái hi?n t?i".
E.3 Frontend displays error message.
E.4 End Use Case.
F. Payroll already locked (Step 7d)
F.1 isLocked = true (may happen from concurrent requests).
F.2 Backend returns 400 error "K? l??ng ?ã b? khóa".
F.3 Frontend displays error message and refreshes view.
F.4 End Use Case.
G. Unauthorized canteen (Step 7b)
G.1 Payroll canteenId doesn't match user's canteenId.
G.2 Backend returns 403 Forbidden.
G.3 Frontend handles error.
G.4 End Use Case.
H. Server error (Step 8-9)
H.1 Database update fails.
H.2 Backend returns 500 error.
H.3 Frontend displays message "L?i khi duy?t k? l??ng".
H.4 End Use Case.Priority:Critical, Must HaveFrequency of Use:Monthly (once per payroll period)Business Rules:? Only payrolls with Calculated status can be approved.
? Approval is irreversible and the payroll becomes immutable.
? After approval, no salary adjustments are allowed.
? All employee salary records must be generated before approval.
? Payroll status must follow the sequence: Draft ? Calculated ? Approved ? Paid.
? If corrections are required after approval, a new payroll must be created.Other Information:? The Approve action is only available when payroll status is "Calculated".
? After approval, the action changes to “Xác nh?n thanh toán” to proceed with payment.
? The Approved status is clearly indicated in the UI.Assumptions:? User has reviewed all salary calculations before approving.
? User has authority to approve payroll for their canteen.
? No further adjustments will be needed after approval.UC31.1 View Activity Logs

UC ID and Name:UC31.1 View Activity LogsCreated By:TraLTCE180100Date Created:06/03/2026Primary Actor:Manager, StaffSecondary Actors:NoneTrigger:The actor accesses the Audit Log page from the Dashboard navigation menu.Description:Allows Manager and Staff to view the basic list of system activity logs for monitoring and security purposes.Preconditions:? The actor has logged into the system.
? The actor has permission to access the Audit Log module (Manager, Staff, or Admin).
? The system has recorded at least one activity log.
? The activity log database is available and functioning normally.Postconditions:? The list of activity logs is fully displayed.
? All necessary log information is displayed accurately (time, action, module, resource, user, description).
? The list is paginated and sorted by the most recent time first.
? No changes are made to the log data.Normal Flow:1. The actor selects the "Audit Log Management" menu from the Dashboard.
2. The system loads the Audit Log page.
3. The system retrieves the activity log list from the database (first page, limit of 20 records, sorted by time in descending order).
4. The system displays the log list with the following columns: Time, Action, Module, Resource, User, Description.
5. The actor views the current list of activity logs.Alternative Flows:A. Change Page (Step 5)
A.1. The actor navigates to another page by selecting a page number or the "Next" button in the pagination section.
A.2. The system loads the log data for the selected page.
A.3. The system updates the list to display 20 records of the new page.
A.4. The actor views the log list of the selected page.
A.5. Return to Step 5.
B. Change Page Size (Step 5)
B.1. The actor selects the number of records displayed per page (10, 20, 50, or 100 records).
B.2. The system updates and reloads the log list with the new record quantity.
B.3. The current page is reset to page 1.
B.4. The system displays the log list with the selected number of records.
B.5. The actor views the updated log list.
B.6. Return to Step 5.Exceptions:E1. Server Error When Loading Audit Logs (Step 3)
E1.1. The system cannot connect to the server or the server returns an error (status code ? 500).
E1.2. The system displays an error message: "Error loading activity logs".
E1.3. The log list remains empty and the actor cannot view the data.
E1.4. The Use Case ends.
E2. No Records Found (Step 4)
E2.1. The system cannot find any activity logs in the database.
E2.2. The system displays an empty list or the message "No activity logs available".
E2.3. The Use Case ends.
E3. Unauthorized Access (Step 2)
E3.1. The actor does not have permission to access the Audit Log module (role is not Manager, Staff, or Admin).
E3.2. The system blocks access and displays the error message: "You do not have permission to access this feature."
E3.3. The actor is redirected to the home page or another permitted page.
E3.4. The Use Case ends.Priority:HighFrequency of Use:Daily – Manager and Staff use this feature daily to review system activities.Business Rules:? Default display of 20 records per page – The log list is paginated for easier management and better performance.
? Sorted by descending time – The newest logs are always displayed first.
? Read-only data – The actor can only view logs and cannot edit or delete them.
? Record all data changes – The system logs CREATE, UPDATE, and DELETE actions for all resources (products, orders, users, etc.).
? Display basic information – The list only shows the most important information: time, action, module, resource, user, and description.Other Information:Related Use Cases: UC-USER-002 (User Management), UC-PRODUCT-001 (Product Management), UC-ORDER-001 (Order Management)
Integration Points: Audit Log Service, Authentication Service, Database
Performance Considerations: The list displays 20 records per page by default and uses pagination to prevent interface overload.Assumptions:? All actors have successfully logged into the system.
? Network connection and server availability are stable.
? Log data is accurately recorded by other system modules.
? Server and client time are synchronized correctly.

The actor has logged into the system with the role of Manager or Staff.
? The actor has permission to access the Inventory Management module.
? The system already contains product and ingredient data.
? The database is operating normally.Postconditions:? The inventory dashboard is fully displayed with tabs (Products, Ingredients).
? Statistics are displayed accurately: number of out-of-stock products, low-stock products, low-stock ingredients, and critical items.
? Detailed lists are loaded into tables according to the current page.
? Inventory status is clearly visualized using colors and icons.
? The actor can manage (update, edit) items directly from the table.Normal Flow:1. The actor selects the "Inventory Management" menu from the Dashboard navigation.
2. The system loads the Inventory Dashboard page.
3. The system sends parallel API requests:
? GET /api/products/inventory/out-of-stock
? GET /api/products/inventory/low-stock
? GET /api/ingredients/low-stock
4. The system calculates statistics: total out-of-stock products, total low-stock products, total low-stock ingredients, and calculates the critical level (? 25% of the warning threshold).
5. The system displays the dashboard with statistic cards at the top: number of out-of-stock products, number of low-stock products, number of low-stock ingredients, and number of critical items.
6. The system displays three tabs: "Out of Stock Products", "Low Stock Products", and "Low Stock Ingredients".
7. By default, the "Out of Stock Products" tab is selected first.
8. The system displays a table listing out-of-stock products with the columns: Product Name, Category, Canteen, Price, Stock (red tag), Warning Threshold, Actions.
9. The actor views the list of out-of-stock products on the current page (page 1, default 10 records per page).Alternative Flows:A. Switch to Low Stock Products Tab (Step 7)
A.1. The actor clicks the "Low Stock Products" tab.
 A.2. The system hides the out-of-stock products table.
 A.3. The system displays the low-stock products table (stockQuantity ? lowStockThreshold and > 0).
 A.4. The table displays columns: Product Name, Category, Canteen, Price, Stock (with color based on percentage), Warning Threshold, % Compared to Threshold, Actions.
 A.5. The statistic card for low-stock products is updated accordingly.
 A.6. Return to Step 9 to view the low-stock product table.
B. Switch to Low Stock Ingredients Tab (Step 7)
B.1. The actor clicks the "Low Stock Ingredients" tab.
 B.2. The system hides the product tables.
 B.3. The system displays the low-stock ingredients table (stock ? lowStockThreshold).
 B.4. The table displays columns: Ingredient Name, Category, Location, Current Stock, Unit, Warning Threshold, % Compared to Threshold, Status, Actions.
 B.5. Statistic cards are displayed at the top: total low-stock ingredients and number of critical items (? 25%).
 B.6. Return to Step 9 to view the low-stock ingredient table.
C. Navigate Pagination (Step 9)
C.1. The actor selects another page or changes the number of records per page.
 C.2. The system sends an API request with the new page and limit.
 C.3. The system updates the table with the selected page data.
 C.4. Pagination information is updated (current page, total pages).
 C.5. Return to Step 9.
D. Edit Product Stock (Step 9)
D.1. The actor clicks the Action (edit icon) on a product record.
 D.2. The "Update Stock" modal opens with product information.
 D.3. The actor enters the quantity change and selects the operation (Add Stock / Deduct Stock / Reset).
 D.4. The actor clicks "Update".
 D.5. The system sends request PATCH /api/products/:id/stock.
 D.6. The server updates the product inventory.
 D.7. The modal closes and the table automatically refreshes.
 D.8. The message "Stock updated successfully" is displayed.
 D.9. Return to Step 9.
E. Edit Ingredient Stock (Step 9 – When in Ingredients Tab)
E.1. The actor is on the "Low Stock Ingredients" tab and clicks the Action button on an ingredient record.
 E.2. The "Update Stock" modal opens with ingredient information.
 E.3. The actor enters the quantity change and selects the operation.
 E.4. The actor clicks "Update".
 E.5. The system sends request PATCH /api/ingredients/:id/stock.
 E.6. The server updates the ingredient inventory.
 E.7. The modal closes and the table automatically refreshes.
 E.8. The message "Stock updated successfully" is displayed.
 E.9. Return to Step 9.Exceptions:E1. Server Error Loading Dashboard Data (Step 3)
E1.1. One or more API requests fail (server error, timeout, connection refused).
 E1.2. The system displays the error message "Unable to load inventory data."
 E1.3. The dashboard still appears but some cards/tables show empty or loading states.
 E1.4. The actor can retry by refreshing the page (F5).
 E1.5. End of Use Case.
E2. No Out Of Stock Products (Step 8)
E2.1. The system successfully retrieves data but the out-of-stock product list is empty.
 E2.2. The system displays an empty state message "No out-of-stock products" with an icon.
 E2.3. The statistic card Out of Stock Products shows 0.
 E2.4. The table remains visible but empty; the user can switch tabs.
 E2.5. Return to Step 7.
E3. No Low Stock Items (Step 8)
E3.1. The system retrieves data successfully but the low-stock list is empty.
 E3.2. The system displays an empty state message "No low-stock products" or "No low-stock ingredients".
 E3.3. The statistic card displays 0.
 E3.4. The user can switch to other tabs to view different data.
 E3.5. Return to Step 7.
E4. Unauthorized Access (Step 2)
E4.1. The actor does not have permission to access the Inventory Management module (invalid role).
 E4.2. The system blocks access and displays the message "You do not have permission to access this feature."
 E4.3. The actor is redirected to the homepage or an authorized page.
 E4.4. End of Use Case.
E5. Update Stock Failed (Step 7 – during D6 or E6)
E5.1. The system sends a stock update request but the server returns an error (validation failed, insufficient stock, etc.).
 E5.2. The system displays the error message "Stock update failed - [reason]."
 E5.3. The modal remains open and the form data is preserved.
 E5.4. The actor can edit and retry or cancel the modal.
 E5.5. Return to Step 9.
E6. Stock Level Changed By Another User (Step 7)
E6.1. The actor is editing stock for a product/ingredient, but before submitting another user updates the same item.
 E6.2. The server detects the conflict and returns an error or warning.
 E6.3. The system displays the message "This data has been updated by another user. Please reload."
 E6.4. The modal closes and the table automatically refreshes with the latest data.
 E6.5. Return to Step 9.Priority:HighFrequency of Use:Daily (whenever searching for specific products)Business Rules:? Canteen-based filtering – Data only shows products and ingredients belonging to the canteen where the staff/manager works.
? Automatic status update – Product status automatically changes to "out_of_stock" when stockQuantity = 0.
? Low stock detection – Items are considered low stock if stock ? lowStockThreshold.
? Critical level – Critical level is defined as stock ? 25% of lowStockThreshold.
? Recipe-based inventory – Products that have recipes use ingredient consumption to calculate inventory instead of relying solely on stockQuantity.
? Real-time updates – When stock is updated, the table automatically refreshes to display the latest data.
? Pagination – Default 10 records per page, with adjustable options.Other Information:Related Use Cases:
? UC-ING-001 (View List Ingredients)
? UC-ING-004 (Update Ingredient)
? UC-PRD-001 (Product Management)
API Endpoints
? GET /api/products/inventory/out-of-stock
? GET /api/products/inventory/low-stock
? GET /api/ingredients/low-stock
? PATCH /api/products/:id/stock
? PATCH /api/ingredients/:id/stock
UI Components
? InventoryDashboard.jsx
? ProductInventoryTab.jsx
? IngredientInventoryTab.jsxAssumptions:? The actor has successfully logged into the system.
? Network connection and server are available.
? Product and ingredient data are stored completely and accurately.
? Each item has a properly configured LowStockThreshold.
? The dashboard uses a tab component that allows easy switching between tabs.
? Each canteen maintains its own inventory data independently.
UC33.1 View List Ingredients

UC ID and Name:UC33.1 View List IngredientsCreated By:TraLTCE180100Date Created:06/03/2026Primary Actor:Manager, StaffSecondary Actors:NoneTrigger:The actor selects "Ingredient Management" from the Dashboard menu.Description:Allows Manager and Staff to view the complete list of ingredients in the system with search, filtering, and pagination capabilities for efficient management.Preconditions:? The actor has logged into the system with the role of Manager or Staff.
? The actor has permission to access the Ingredient Management module.
? The system already contains at least one ingredient in the database.
? The database is operating normally.Postconditions:? The ingredient list is displayed according to the current page.
? All information is displayed correctly: name, category, stock, unit, warning threshold, status, and created date.
? Ingredients with low stock are marked with a red tag.
? Pagination correctly displays the total number of ingredients and pages.
? No changes are made to the data (read-only).Normal Flow:1. The actor selects the "Ingredient Management" menu from the Dashboard.
2. The system loads the Ingredient Management page.
3. The system sends an API request GET /api/ingredients with default parameters (page=1, limit=10).
4. The system receives data from the server and displays the ingredient list table with the following columns: Ingredient Name, Category, Stock, Unit, Warning Threshold, Status, Created Date, Actions.
5. The system displays pagination information at the bottom of the table (current page/total pages, total ingredients).
6. The actor views the ingredient list on the current page.
7. The system highlights ingredients with stock ? lowStockThreshold using a red tag with the text "Low Stock".Alternative Flows:A. Change Page (Step 6)
A.1. The actor selects another page in the pagination control (page number or Next/Previous button).
A.2. The system sends an API request with the new page parameter.
A.3. The system updates the table with the data of the selected page.
A.4. Return to Step 6.
B. Change Page Size (Step 6)
B.1. The actor selects the number of records displayed per page (10, 20, 50, 100).
B.2. The system sends an API request with a new limit parameter and resets page=1.
B.3. The system updates the table with the new number of records.
B.4. Return to Step 6.
C. Sort by Column (Step 6)
C.1. The actor clicks on a sortable column header (Name, Stock, Created Date).
C.2. The system sorts the data by the selected column (ascending or descending).
C.3. The table is updated with the new sorting order.
C.4. Return to Step 6.Exceptions:E1. Server Error (Step 3)
E1.1. The system cannot connect to the server or the server returns an error (status ? 500).
E1.2. The system displays an error message: "Cannot load ingredient list."
E1.3. The table remains empty or displays previously cached data (if available).
E1.4. The Use Case ends.
E2. No Data Available (Step 4)
E2.1. The server returns an empty list (no ingredients available).
E2.2. The system displays an empty table with the message "No data available."
E2.3. The Use Case ends.
E3. Unauthorized Access (Step 2)
E3.1. The actor does not have permission to access the module (invalid role).
E3.2. The system displays an error message: "You do not have permission to access this feature."
E3.3. The actor is redirected to the home page.
E3.4. The Use Case ends.Priority:HighFrequency of Use:Daily (when reviewing detailed product information)Business Rules:? Only display ingredients of the current canteen – Data is filtered based on the user's canteenId.
? Read-only operation – This use case only reads data and does not modify it.
? Stock warning visualization – Ingredients with stock ? lowStockThreshold are highlighted in red.
? Default pagination – By default, 10 records are displayed per page.
? Active status display – Status is displayed as Active/Suspended using green/gray tags.Other Information:Related Use Cases: UC-ING-002 (Detail Ingredient), UC-ING-003 (Add Ingredient), UC-ING-004 (Update Ingredient), UC-ING-005 (Filter Ingredient)
API Endpoint: GET /api/ingredients
UI Component: IngredientListView.jsxAssumptions:? The actor has successfully logged into the system.
? Network connection and server availability are stable.
? Ingredient data is stored completely and accurately.
? The browser fully supports the features of Ant Design Table.
UC33.2 View Ingredient Detail

UC ID and Name:UC33.2 View Ingredient DetailCreated By:TraLTCE180100Date Created:06/03/2026Primary Actor:Manager, StaffSecondary Actors:NoneTrigger:The Manager or Staff selects to view the details of an ingredient from the ingredient list.Description:This use case allows the Manager or Staff to view the complete detailed information of a specific ingredient in the system, including basic information, stock level, category, and activity status.Preconditions:? The actor has logged into the system with the role of Manager or Staff.
? The actor has been authenticated and has permission to access the ingredient management module.
? The Ingredient ID is valid and exists in the system.
? The database system is functioning normally.Postconditions:? The detailed ingredient information is fully displayed on the screen.
? The system records the detail view activity (if an audit log exists).
? The actor can perform further actions such as editing or returning to the ingredient list.Normal Flow:1. The actor accesses the ingredient list screen.
2. The actor selects a specific ingredient to view its details.
3. The system receives the request containing the Ingredient ID from the actor.
4. The system verifies the actor’s access permissions and validates the Ingredient ID.
5. The system queries the ingredient details from the database.
6. The system populates related information (Canteen, Category).
7. The system calculates the stock warning status (isLowStock).
8. The system displays the complete ingredient information including: ingredient name, stock quantity, unit, category, canteen, low stock threshold, activity status, creation time, and last update time.
9. The actor views the detailed ingredient information.
10. The use case ends successfully.Alternative Flows:A. Viewing an ingredient with low stock (Step 8)
A.1 The system detects that the ingredient stock is less than or equal to the lowStockThreshold.
A.2 The system displays a red/orange warning indicator with a low stock alert icon.
A.3 The system suggests the quantity that should be restocked based on lowStockThreshold.
A.4 The actor views the ingredient details with the warning notification.
A.5 Return to Step 9 of the Normal Flow.
B. Viewing an inactive ingredient (Step 8)
B.1 The system detects that the ingredient has isActive = false.
B.2 The system displays an “Inactive” badge on the interface.
B.3 The system disables buttons related to stock modification or recipe usage.
B.4 The actor views the ingredient information with inactive status.
B.5 Return to Step 9 of the Normal Flow.
C. Refreshing the latest information (Step 9)
C.1 The actor clicks the “Refresh” button while viewing the details.
C.2 The system sends another request to retrieve the latest data from the database.
C.3 Return to Step 5 of the Normal Flow.Exceptions:E. Ingredient not found (Step 5)
E.1 The system cannot find the ingredient with the provided ID.
E.2 The system returns a 404 error with the message “Ingredient not found”.
E.3 The system displays an error notification to the actor.
E.4 The actor is redirected back to the ingredient list screen.
E.5 The use case ends unsuccessfully.
F. Database connection error (Step 5)
F.1 The system cannot connect to or query the database.
F.2 The system records detailed error logs in the system log.
F.3 The system displays the message “System error, please try again later”.
F.4 The actor can choose to retry or go back.
F.5 The use case ends unsuccessfully.
G. Invalid Ingredient ID (Step 4)
G.1 The system detects that the Ingredient ID does not match the MongoDB ObjectId format.
G.2 The system returns a 400 Bad Request error.
G.3 The system displays the message “Invalid Ingredient ID”.
G.4 The actor returns to the ingredient list screen.
G.5 The use case ends unsuccessfully.Priority:HighFrequency of Use:Multiple times per day (whenever the Manager/Staff needs to check ingredient details)Business Rules:? Only Manager and Staff are allowed to view ingredient details.
? The detail information must include all fields: name, stock, unit, categoryId, canteenId, lowStockThreshold, isActive.
? The virtual field isLowStock is dynamically calculated based on the comparison between stock and lowStockThreshold.
? Canteen and Category information must be populated to display names instead of only IDs.
? Ingredients with isActive = false cannot be used in new recipes.Other Information:API Endpoint: GET /api/v1/ingredients/:id
Response Format: JSON with status code 200 (success) or 404 (not found)
Performance Requirement: Query response time must be under 500ms
The system uses populate to eager-load Canteen and Category information.
Timestamp fields (createdAt, updatedAt) are automatically managed by MongoDB.Assumptions:? The actor has basic knowledge of inventory and ingredient management.
? The network connection between client and server is stable.
? The MongoDB database is running and contains sufficient reference data (Canteen, Category).
? The ingredient has been created previously and has a valid ID.
? The actor accesses the system from a device with a modern web browser that supports JavaScript.
UC33.3 Add Ingredient

UC ID and Name:UC33.3 Add IngredientCreated By:TraLTCE180100Date Created:24/0/2026Primary Actor:Manager, StaffSecondary Actors:noneTrigger:The actor clicks the "Add Ingredient" button on the ingredient list page.Description:Allows Manager and Staff to add a new ingredient to the system with all required information.Preconditions:The actor has logged into the system.
The actor is currently on the ingredient list page.
The system already contains at least one ingredient category.
The actor has permission to create a new ingredient.Postconditions:The new ingredient is created and stored in the database.
The ingredient list refreshes to display the new record.
A success notification is displayed.
An audit log is recorded with the CREATE action.
The modal form is closed.
Normal Flow:1. The actor clicks the "Add Ingredient" button at the top-right of the card.
2. The system opens the "Add New Ingredient" modal.
3. The system displays a form with the following fields:
? Ingredient Name (required)
? Category (required, dropdown)
? Unit (required, select from 9 valid values)
? Stock (default: 0)
? Warning Threshold (default: 10)
? Status (switch, default: Active)
4. The actor fills in the required information in the form.
5. The actor clicks the "Create" button.
6. The system validates all fields according to the rules (required, max length, minimum value).
7. The system sends an API request POST /api/ingredients with a payload including canteenId from the user context.
8. The server creates the new ingredient in the database.
9. The server records an audit log with the CREATE action.
10. The system receives a successful response from the server.
11. The system displays the notification: "Ingredient added successfully."
12. The modal closes and the form is reset.
13. The ingredient list automatically refreshes to display the new record.Alternative Flows:A. Cancel Add (Step 5)
A.1. The actor clicks the "Cancel" button or clicks outside the modal.
A.2. The system displays a confirmation dialog (if data has been entered).
A.3. The actor confirms the cancellation.
A.4. The modal closes and the form is reset.
A.5. No changes are made to the data.
A.6. The Use Case ends.
B. Validation Error (Step 6)
B.1. The system detects validation errors (missing required fields, invalid format).
B.2. The system displays error messages below the invalid fields.
B.3. The system displays a toast message: "Please check the entered information."
B.4. Return to Step 4 for the actor to correct the input.Exceptions:E1. Duplicate Ingredient Name (Step 8)
E1.1. The server detects that the ingredient name already exists within the same canteen (unique constraint).
E1.2. The server returns a 400 error with the message: "Ingredient name already exists."
E1.3. The system displays the error message: "Ingredient name already exists."
E1.4. The modal remains open so the actor can modify the name and try again.
E1.5. Return to Step 4.
E2. Server Error (Step 7)
E2.1. The server encounters an error while processing the request (database error, validation error).
E2.2. The system displays the error message: "Failed to add ingredient."
E2.3. The modal remains open and the form data is preserved.
E2.4. The actor may retry or cancel the operation.
E2.5. The Use Case ends.
E3. Category Not Found (Step 3)
E3.1. The system cannot load the category list (API error).
E3.2. The category dropdown appears empty or shows an error state.
E3.3. The system displays the message: "Unable to load ingredient categories."
E3.4. The actor cannot submit the form.
E3.5. The Use Case ends.Priority:HighFrequency of Use:Weekly – Used when new ingredients need to be added.Business Rules:? Unique name per canteen – Ingredient names must be unique within the same canteen.
? Valid units only – Only 9 units are allowed: kg, g, liter, lít, ml, piece, pack, box, can.
? Auto-assign canteenId – The canteenId is retrieved from the user context and cannot be manually selected.
? Default values – Stock default is 0, lowStockThreshold is 10, and isActive is true.
? Name length limit – Ingredient names must not exceed 100 characters.
? Non-negative stock – Stock and lowStockThreshold must not be negative.
? Audit logging – The system records creator information, timestamp, and the newly created data.Other Information:Related Use Cases: UC-ING-001 (View List)
API Endpoint: POST /api/ingredients
UI Component: IngredientFormModal.jsx (mode: create)Assumptions:? The category list is loaded before opening the modal.
? The user context contains a valid canteenId.
? Form validation is performed on both client-side and server-side.

UC33.4 Update Ingredient 

UC ID and Name:UC33.4 Update Ingredient Created By:TraLTCE180100Date Created:06/03/2026Primary Actor:Manager, StaffSecondary Actors:Cloudinary (Image Management)Trigger:The actor clicks the "Edit" button in the dropdown menu of an ingredient record.Description:Allows Manager and Staff to update information of an existing ingredient, including name, category, unit, stock, warning threshold, and status.Preconditions:The actor has logged into the system.
The actor is currently on the ingredient list page.
The ingredient to be edited exists in the system.
The actor has permission to update ingredients.Postconditions:? The ingredient information is updated in the database.
? The ingredient list refreshes to display the updated data.
? A success notification is displayed.
? The audit log records the UPDATE action with oldValues and newValues.
? The modal form is closed.Normal Flow:1. The actor clicks the "Action" dropdown menu on an ingredient record.
2. The actor selects "Edit".
3. The system opens the "Edit Ingredient" modal.
4. The system loads the current ingredient data into the form.
5. The actor modifies one or more fields (name, category, unit, stock, warning threshold, status).
6. The actor clicks the "Update" button.
7. The system validates all fields according to the defined rules.
8. The system sends an API request PATCH /api/ingredients/:id with the updated data.
9. The server updates the ingredient in the database.
10. The server records an audit log with action UPDATE, storing oldValues and newValues.
11. The system receives a successful response from the server.
12. The system displays the notification: "Ingredient updated successfully."
13. The modal closes and the form is reset.
14. The ingredient list automatically refreshes to display the updated data.Alternative Flows:A. Cancel Update (Step 6)
A.1. The actor clicks the "Cancel" button or clicks outside the modal.
A.2. The system displays a confirmation dialog if changes were made.
A.3. The actor confirms cancellation.
A.4. The modal closes and the form is reset.
A.5. No data changes occur.
A.6. The Use Case ends.
B. No Changes Made (Step 6)
B.1. The actor does not modify any fields and clicks "Update".
B.2. The system still sends the update request to the server.
B.3. The server confirms no changes but returns a success response.
B.4. The system displays a success notification.
B.5. Return to Step 13.
C. Update from Detail Modal (Step 2)
C.1. The actor is currently viewing the Ingredient Detail modal (UC-ING-002).
C.2. The actor clicks the "Edit" button in the modal footer.
C.3. The detail modal closes.
C.4. The system opens the "Edit Ingredient" modal with data already loaded from the detail view.
C.5. Return to Step 5Exceptions:E1. Duplicate Name Conflict (Step 9)
E1.1. The server detects that the new name duplicates another ingredient within the same canteen.
E1.2. The server returns a 400 error with the message: "Ingredient name already exists."
E1.3. The system displays the error message.
E1.4. The modal remains open so the actor can correct the name and retry.
E1.5. Return to Step 5.
E2. Ingredient Not Found (Step 9)
E2.1. The ingredient has been deleted by another user while the actor is editing.
E2.2. The server returns a 404 error.
E2.3. The system displays the notification: "Ingredient not found."
E2.4. The modal closes automatically.
E2.5. The ingredient list refreshes to remove the invalid record.
E2.6. The Use Case ends.
E3. Validation Error (Step 7)
E3.1. The input data is invalid (name too long, negative stock, etc.).
E3.2. The system displays validation error messages.
E3.3. The modal remains open.
E3.4. Return to Step 5 for the actor to correct the data.
E4. Server Error (Step 8)
E4.1. The server encounters an error while processing the request.
E4.2. The system displays the notification: "Failed to update ingredient."
E4.3. The modal remains open and the form data is preserved.
E4.4. The actor may retry or cancel the operation.
E4.5. The Use Case ends.Priority:HighFrequency of Use:Daily (price updates, inventory updates, status changes)Business Rules:? Preserve unique constraint – The updated name must not duplicate another ingredient within the same canteen.
? Cannot change canteenId – The canteenId cannot be modified after creation.
? Audit old and new values – The audit log must store both previous and updated values to track changes.
? Validation same as create – All validation rules applied in the create use case are also applied here.
? Update timestamp – The updatedAt field is automatically updated.Other Information:Related Use Cases: UC-ING-001 (View List), UC-ING-002 (Detail Ingredient)
API Endpoint: PATCH /api/ingredients/:id
UI Component: IngredientFormModal.jsx (mode: edit)Assumptions:? The form is pre-filled with the current ingredient data.
? Optimistic UI update is applied after receiving a successful response.
UC33.5 Filter Ingredient 

UC ID and Name:UC33.5 Filter IngredientCreated By:TraLTCE180100Date Created:06/03/2026Primary Actor:Manager, StaffSecondary Actors:NoneTrigger:The actor uses the filter controls on the ingredient list page.Description:Allows Managers and Staff to filter the ingredient list based on criteria such as name search, category filter, and stock status filter in order to easily search and manage ingredients.Preconditions:? The actor has logged into the system.
? The actor is on the ingredient list page (UC-ING-001).
? The system already contains ingredient and category data.Postconditions:? The ingredient list is filtered according to the selected criteria.
? Pagination is reset to page 1 with the updated total number of records.
? Filter state is stored in the session.
? The counter “Total X ingredients” reflects the number of records after filtering.Normal Flow:1. The actor is viewing the full ingredient list (all records).
2. The actor enters a keyword in the “Search ingredient” field and presses Enter or clicks the search button.
3. The system sends an API request GET /api/ingredients with the query parameter search=keyword.
4. The server searches for ingredients whose names contain the keyword (case-insensitive).
5. The system receives the results and updates the list table.
6. Pagination is reset to page 1 with the updated number of records.
7. The actor views the list filtered by the search keyword.Alternative Flows:A. Filter by Category (Step 2)
A.1. The actor selects a category from the “Filter by category” dropdown.
A.2. The system sends an API request with the query parameter categoryId.
A.3. The server filters ingredients by categoryId.
A.4. The system updates the list with the filtered results.
A.5. Return to Step 6.
B. Filter by Stock Status (Step 2)
B.1. The actor selects a stock status from the “Filter by stock” dropdown (All / Low Stock / In Stock).
 B.2. If Low Stock is selected: the system sends the request and filters client-side for ingredients with stock <= lowStockThreshold.
 B.3. If In Stock is selected: the system filters ingredients with stock > lowStockThreshold.
 B.4. If All is selected: the system removes the stock filter.
 B.5. The list is updated with the filtered results.
 B.6. Return to Step 6.
C. Combine Multiple Filters (Step 2)
C.1. The actor applies multiple filters simultaneously (search + category + stock status).
 C.2. The system sends a request with all query parameters.
 C.3. The server and client-side filters combine all conditions using AND logic.
 C.4. The list displays only records that satisfy all conditions.
 C.5. Return to Step 6.
D. Reset Filters (Step 7)
D.1. The actor clicks the “Reset” button.
 D.2. The system clears all filter values (search, categoryId, stockStatus).
 D.3. The system sends a request with the default query (no filters).
 D.4. The list displays all ingredients.
 D.5. Return to Step 1.
E. Clear Individual Filter (Step 7)
E.1. The actor clicks the “X” button on a filter dropdown or clears the search text.
 E.2. The system removes that filter while keeping other filters unchanged.
 E.3. The list is updated with the remaining filter conditions.
 E.4. Return to Step 6.Exceptions:E1. No Results Found (Step 5)
E1.1. No ingredients match the filtering conditions.
 E1.2. The system displays an empty table with the message “No data available.”
 E1.3. The filters remain unchanged.
 E1.4. The actor can adjust filters or reset them.
 E1.5. End of Use Case.
E2. Server Error (Step 3)
E2.1. The server does not respond or returns an error.
 E2.2. The system displays the message “Unable to load ingredient list.”
 E2.3. The list displays previous data or remains empty.
 E2.4. The actor may retry or reset filters.
 E2.5. End of Use Case.Priority:HighFrequency of Use:Daily (menu updates and management activities)Business Rules:? Case-insensitive search – Search is not case-sensitive.
? Partial match – Search uses a contains condition, not an exact full-name match.
? AND logic for multiple filters – When combining filters, apply AND logic.
? Client-side stock filtering – Stock status filtering is handled on the client side.
? Preserve pagination – When filters change, pagination resets to page 1.
? Real-time filtering – Filter results are applied immediately when a filter changes.Other Information:Related Use Cases: UC-ING-001 (View List)
API Endpoint: GET /api/ingredients (with query parameters)
UI Components: IngredientListView.jsx, useIngredientManagement.jsAssumptions:? Filter state is managed by React hooks.
? URL query parameters are not used (state is stored only in memory).
? The category list has already been loaded.
? Filters are applied immediately when the actor changes a selection (no Apply button required).

UC38.1 Review Registration

UC ID and Name:UC38.1 Review RegistrationCreated By:DuyQKCE182413Date Created:02/02/2026Primary Actor:Admin SystemSecondary Actors:NoneTrigger:Admin clicks Approve or Reject in Pending Canteens tab.Description:Allows Admin to review a pending canteen registration and set final status.Preconditions:? User is authenticated.
? User role is admin.
? Canteen exists and status is pending.Postconditions:? Canteen status is updated: approve -> active, reject -> inactive.
? If rejected, related manager accounts are set to inactive.Normal Flow:1. Admin opens Canteen Governance page.
2. Frontend loads pending canteens via GET /api/canteens?status=pending.
3. Admin clicks Approve or Reject in row action.
4. Frontend sends PATCH /api/canteens/:id/review with body { decision: 'approve' } or { decision: 'reject' }.
5. Backend middleware protect validates JWT.
6. Backend middleware restrictTo('admin') validates role.
7. Controller validates decision value.
8. Service checks canteen existence and current pending status.
9. Service updates canteen status (approve -> active, reject -> inactive).
10. If decision is reject, service updates linked manager users to inactive.
11. Backend returns HTTP 200 with reviewed canteen payload.
12. Frontend reloads active and pending lists, then shows success message.Alternative Flows:A. Approve path (Step 3)
A.1 Admin clicks Approve.
A.2 System sets canteen status to active.
A.3 Record moves from pending list after reload.
B. Reject path (Step 3)
B.1 Admin clicks Reject.
B.2 System sets canteen status to inactive and deactivates linked manager account(s).
B.3 Record is removed from pending list after reload.
C. Admin does not perform review (Step 3)
C.1 Admin only views list and takes no action.
C.2 No review API call is sent.
C.3 Data remains unchanged.Exceptions:E. Invalid decision (Step 7)
E.1 Request body has unsupported decision value.
E.2 Backend returns 400.
E.3 Frontend shows error message.
F. Canteen not found (Step 8)
F.1 Canteen ID does not exist.
F.2 Backend returns 404.
F.3 Frontend shows error and ends action.
G. Canteen is not pending (Step 8)
G.1 Target canteen status is not pending.
G.2 Backend returns 400.
G.3 Frontend keeps current list state.
H. Unauthorized/Forbidden (Step 5-6)
H.1 JWT missing/expired/invalid -> 401.
H.2 Non-admin user -> 403.
H.3 Frontend handles auth/permission error.Priority:HighFrequency of Use:FrequentBusiness Rules:? Can only view details of ingredient categories belonging to own canteen (backend filters by canteenId).
? Detail GET API is public but backend still checks canteenId to ensure data isolation.
? If description, createdAt or updatedAt fields are empty, display "—" character.Other Information:? Only admin can review registration.
? Only pending canteens are reviewable.
? Reject operation has side effect on manager account status.Assumptions:

UC39.3 Create System Notification

UC ID and Name:UC39.3 Create System NotificationCreated By:LocMTCE181795Date Created:07/03/2026Primary Actor:AdminSecondary Actors:NoneTrigger:Actor clicks “Create Notification” button and submits the form.Description:This Use Case allows Admin and Manager to create a new system notification. The notification can be targeted to specific roles or scopes (global or canteen). The system ensures validation of input data, applies role-based constraints, and optionally dispatches the notification through realtime or bulk mechanisms.Preconditions:? Actor is authenticated and authorized (admin or manager).
? Required fields including title and content must be provided.
? Manager must be associated with a valid canteenId.
? System notification service is available.Postconditions:? A new system notification record is created in the database.
? Notification is optionally dispatched to users.
? Frontend updates notification list.Normal Flow:1. Actor clicks Create Notification button.
2. System displays notification creation form.
3. Actor enters notification details including title, content, targetRole, activeFrom, activeTo, and optional scope.
4. Actor submits the form.
5. Frontend sends POST /api/notifications/system request with payload and Bearer Token.
6. Backend verifies authentication and authorization.
7. Controller calls notificationService.createSystemNotification.
8. Service validates required fields (title, content).
9. Service validates role-based constraints.
10. Service normalizes payload based on actor role:
11. Admin can set any scope.
12. Manager is restricted to their own canteen.
13. Service creates SystemNotification record with createdBy field.
14. Service determines dispatch strategy.
15. Service triggers notification dispatch (bulk or realtime).
16. Backend returns HTTP 201 with created notification data.
17. Frontend displays success message.
18. Frontend refreshes notification list.Alternative Flows:A. Manager scope enforcement (Step 10)
A.1 Manager provides custom canteenId.
A.2 System overrides canteenId with actor’s canteenId.
B. No dispatch required (Step 12)
B.1 Notification is saved without triggering bulk dispatch.
B.2 System emits realtime event only.
C. Cancel creation (Step 3)
C.1 Actor closes form without submitting.
C.2 System discards input data.
C.3 Use Case ends.Exceptions:E. Missing required fields (Step 8)
E.1 Title or content is missing.
E.2 Backend returns HTTP 400.
F. Invalid target role (Step 9)
F.1 Manager sets targetRole as admin or manager.
F.2 Backend returns HTTP 400.
G. Unauthorized actor (Step 6)
G.1 Actor not permitted to create notification.
G.2 Backend returns HTTP 403.
H. Dispatch failure (Step 13)
H.1 Notification dispatch process fails.
H.2 System logs error and continues response.Priority:High, Must HaveFrequency of Use:ModerateBusiness Rules:? Manager cannot create notifications targeting admin or manager roles.
? Admin can create both global and canteen notifications.
? Default targetRole is "all" if not provided.
? Notification must contain non-empty title and content.Other Information:? System supports realtime notification via socket or event mechanism.Assumptions:? Notification dispatch service is available and operational.
? Frontend validates input before submission.
UC39.4 Update System Notification

UC ID and Name:UC39.4 Update System NotificationCreated By:LocMTCE181795Date Created:07/03/2026Primary Actor:AdminSecondary Actors:NoneTrigger:Actor edits an existing notification and clicks the Save button.Description:This Use Case allows Admin and Manager to update information of an existing system notification. The system validates input data, enforces role-based restrictions, and ensures that only notifications within writable scope can be modified.Preconditions:? Actor is authenticated and authorized.
? Notification exists in the system.
? Notification is within actor’s writable scope.
? Input data is valid.Postconditions:? Notification is updated with new values.
? Updated data is reflected in the system.Normal Flow:1. Actor selects a notification to edit.
2. System displays edit form with current data.
3. Actor modifies fields such as title, content, targetRole, active status, or active period.
4. Actor clicks Save.
5. Frontend sends PATCH /api/notifications/system/:id request.
6. Backend verifies authentication and authorization.
7. Controller calls notificationService.updateSystemNotification.
8. Service validates writable scope based on actor role.
9. Service validates input data (non-empty title/content if provided).
10. Service normalizes payload based on role constraints.
11. Service updates notification using database operation.
12. Backend returns HTTP 200 with updated notification data.
13. Frontend displays success message.
14. Frontend refreshes detail or list view.Alternative Flows:A. Cancel update (Step 3)
A.1 Actor closes edit form without saving.
A.2 System discards changes.
A.3 Use Case ends.
B. Partial update (Step 3)
B.1 Actor updates only some fields.
B.2 System validates only provided fields.Exceptions:E. Notification not found (Step 8)
E.1 Notification does not exist or out of scope.
E.2 Backend returns HTTP 404.
F. Invalid input data (Step 9)
F.1 Title or content is empty string.
F.2 Backend returns HTTP 400.
G. Invalid target role (Step 10)
G.1 Manager sets targetRole as admin or manager.
G.2 Backend returns HTTP 400.
H. Token invalid (Step 6)
H.1 Authentication fails.
H.2 Backend returns HTTP 401.Priority:High, Must HaveFrequency of Use:ModerateBusiness Rules:? Manager can only update notifications within their own canteen.
? Deleted notifications cannot be updated.
? Manager cannot modify canteenId outside their scope.Other Information:? Update operation uses validation with runValidators enabled.Assumptions:? Frontend sends only editable fields.
UC39.5 Delete System Notification

UC ID and Name:UC39.5 Delete System NotificationCreated By:LocMTCE181795Date Created:07/03/2026Primary Actor:AdminSecondary Actors:NoneTrigger:Actor selects delete action and confirms deletion.Description:This Use Case allows Admin and Manager to delete a system notification using a soft delete mechanism. Instead of removing the record from the database, the system marks it as deleted for audit and traceability purposes.Preconditions:? Actor is authenticated and authorized.
? Notification exists and is within actor’s writable scope.Postconditions:? Notification is marked as deleted (isDeleted = true).
? Notification is no longer visible in standard queries.Normal Flow:1. Actor clicks Delete button on a notification.
2. System displays confirmation dialog.
3. Actor confirms deletion.
4. Frontend sends DELETE /api/notifications/system/:id request.
5. Backend verifies authentication and authorization.
6. Controller calls notificationService.deleteSystemNotification.
7. Service validates writable scope.
8. Service performs soft delete:
? isDeleted = true
? deletedAt = current time
? isActive = false
9. Service saves updated record.
10. Backend returns HTTP 204 (No Content).
11. Frontend removes notification from list.
12. Frontend displays success message.Alternative Flows:A. Cancel deletion (Step 2)
A.1 Actor cancels confirmation dialog.
A.2 System aborts delete action.
A.3 Use Case ends.Exceptions:E. Notification not found (Step 7)
E.1 Notification does not exist or out of scope.
E.2 Backend returns HTTP 404.
F. Token invalid (Step 5)
F.1 Authentication fails.
F.2 Backend returns HTTP 401.
G. Unauthorized role (Step 5)
G.1 Actor does not have permission.
G.2 Backend returns HTTP 403.
H. Network failure (Step 4)
H.1 Request fails due to connectivity issue.
H.2 Frontend displays error message.Priority:High, Must HaveFrequency of Use:WeeklyBusiness Rules:? Deletion is logical (soft delete), not physical removal.
? Deleted notifications are excluded from all list and detail queries.
? Manager can only delete notifications within their own canteen.Other Information:? System maintains deleted records for audit logging and history tracking.Assumptions:? Frontend always confirms deletion before sending request.

UC41.1 View List Permissions

UC ID and Name:UC41.1_View List PermissionsCreated By:ThaiTQCE181618Date Created:24/3/2026Primary Actor:Admin SystemSecondary Actors:NoneTrigger:An admin user wants to view the list of permissions in the system.Description:This Use Case allows the Admin System to view all permissions for administration and role assignment.Preconditions:? 2. The actor requests to load the permission list.
3. The UI sends a GET `/api/roles/permissions` request with JWT token.
4. The system validates the token and admin role.
5. The system queries `Permission` collection and sorts by `code` ascending.
6. The system returns the permissions list.
7. The UI displays the permission list (code, description, createdAt).Alternative Flows:A. No permissions exist (Step 5–7)
   A.1 The system returns an empty array.
   A.2 The UI displays an empty list state.

   B.1 The system rejects the request with 403 Forbidden.
   B.2 The UI displays an authorization error message.
   B.3 End Use Case.Exceptions:E. Network connection lost (Step 3)
   E.1 Network connection is lost while sending the request.
   E.2 The system/UI displays a network error message.
   E.3 End Use Case.
F. Unknown system error (Step 6–7)
   F.1 An unexpected error occurs while querying canteen information.
   F.2 The system logs the error.
   F.3 The system returns a generic error message.
   F.4 End Use Case.Priority:MediumFrequency of Use:ModerateBusiness Rules:? Only admin can access permission management APIs.
? Permissions are ordered by `code` to ensure stable UI rendering.Other Information:Used as the data source for assigning permissions to roles.Assumptions:Permission data may already exist (seeded) or can be empty initially.
UC41.2 View Permission Detail

UC ID and Name:UC41.2_View Permission DetailCreated By:ThaiTQCE181618Date Created:24/3/2026Primary Actor:Admin SystemSecondary Actors:NoneTrigger:The admin user wants to inspect a specific permission and see which roles are using it.Description:This Use Case allows the Admin System to view the detail of a permission, including the roles currently assigned to that permission.Preconditions:? The actor is logged in.<br>
? The actor has admin privileges.
? At least one permission exists (otherwise UC26.1 will show empty list).Postconditions:The selected permission detail is displayed, including `roleCount` and the list of roles using it.Normal Flow:1. The actor opens Permission Management (UC26.1 completed).
2. The actor clicks “Detail” on a permission row.
The UI sends GET `/api/roles/permissions/:id` with JWT token.
3. The system validates the token and admin role.
4. The system finds the permission by id in `Permission` collection.
5. The system queries `RolePermission` mappings for that permission, populates `roleId`, and builds `roles` + `roleCount`.
6. The system returns the permission detail object.
The UI displays the detail modal (code, description, roleCount, roles).Alternative Flows:A. Permission not found (Step 5)
   A.1 The permission id does not exist or is deleted.
   A.2 The system returns 404 Not Found (“Permission not found”).
   A.3 The UI displays an error and closes/keeps the detail view empty.
   A.4 End Use Case.
B. Permission exists but no roles use it (Step 6–8)
   B.1 The system returns `roles: []` and `roleCount: 0`.
   B.2 The UI displays “No roles assigned” state.
   B.3 End Use Case.Exceptions:E. Network connection lost (Step 3)
   E.1 Network connection is lost while sending the request.
   E.2 The system/UI displays a network error message.
   E.3 End Use Case.

F. Unknown system error (Step 5-6)
   F.1 An unexpected error occurs while querying canteen information.
   F.2 The system logs the error.
   F.3 The system returns a generic error message.
   F.4 End Use Case.Priority:MediumFrequency of Use:ModerateBusiness Rules:? Only admin can access this feature.
? Role list is derived from `RolePermission` mappings.Other Information:Supports administration and impact analysis before editing/deleting permissions.Assumptions:Role-permission mappings may exist or be empty.
UC41.3 Create Permission

UC ID and Name:UC41.3_Create PermissionCreated By:ThaiTQCE181618Date Created:24/3/2026Primary Actor:Admin SystemSecondary Actors:NoneTrigger:The admin user wants to create a new permission code for feature access control.Description:This Use Case allows the Admin System to create a new permission in the system.Preconditions:? The actor is logged in.
? The actor has admin privileges.Postconditions:A new permission record is created and appears in the permission list.Normal Flow:1. The actor opens Permission Management screen.
2. The actor clicks “Create new permission”.
3. The system displays the create form (code, description).
4. The actor enters a permission code and optional description.
5. The actor clicks “Create”.
6. The UI sends POST `/api/roles/permissions` with body `{ code, description? }` and JWT token.
7. The system validates the token and admin role.
8. The system creates a new `Permission` document.
9. The system returns HTTP 201 with the created permission.
10. The UI displays a success message and refreshes the permission list.Alternative Flows:A. Duplicate permission code (Step 8–10)
   A.1 The code already exists (unique index conflict).
   A.2 The system returns 400 Bad Request (duplicate key error transformed by global error handler).
   A.3 The UI displays an error and keeps the form open.
   A.4 Return to Step 4.

B. Invalid code format (Step 4–6)
   B.1 The actor enters an invalid code (client validation may prevent it).
   B.2 The UI shows validation errors and blocks submission.
   B.3 Return to Step 4.Exceptions:E. Network connection lost (Step 6)
   E.1 Network connection is lost while sending the request.
   E.2 The system/UI displays a network error message.
   E.3 End Use Case.

F. Unknown system error (Step 8)
   F.1 An unexpected error occurs while querying canteen information.
   F.2 The system logs the error.
   F.3 The system returns a generic error message.
   F.4 End Use Case.Priority:HighFrequency of Use:Low to ModerateBusiness Rules:? Permission code must be unique.
? Permission code is stored in uppercase by schema.
? Only admin can create permissions.
? Create action is audited via `auditLogger('CREATE','Permission','Permission')`.Other Information:After creation, the permission can be assigned to roles (UC26.5).Assumptions:The admin user knows the naming convention for permission codes.
UC41.4 Update Permission

UC ID and Name:UC41.4_Update PermissionCreated By:ThaiTQCE181618Date Created:24/3/2026Primary Actor:Admin SystemSecondary Actors:NoneTrigger:The admin user wants to create a new permission code for feature access control.Description:This Use Case allows the Admin System to edit a permission’s information to keep access control definitions consistent.Preconditions:? The actor is logged in.
? The actor has admin privileges.
? The permission to be edited exists.Postconditions:The permission is updated and the updated value is displayed in the list/detail.Normal Flow:1. The actor opens Permission Management (UC26.1 completed).
2. The actor clicks “Edit” for a permission.
3. The system displays the edit form pre-filled with current data.
4. The actor changes fields (code and/or description).
5. The actor clicks “Update”.
6. The UI sends PATCH `/api/roles/permissions/:id` with updated body and JWT token.
7. The system validates the token and admin role.
8. The system updates the `Permission` document with validation.
9. The system returns HTTP 200 with the updated permission.
10. The UI shows success message and refreshes the permission list.Alternative Flows:A. Permission not found (Step 8)
   A.1 The permission id no longer exists.
   A.2 The system returns 404 Not Found.
   A.3 The UI shows an error and refreshes the list.
   A.4 End Use Case.

B. Update causes duplicate code (Step 8–10)
   B.1 The new code conflicts with another permission.
   B.2 The system returns 400 Bad Request (duplicate key error).
   B.3 The UI displays error and keeps the edit form open.
   B.4 Return to Step 4.Exceptions:E. Network connection lost (Step 6)
   E.1 Network connection is lost while sending the request.
   E.2 The system/UI displays a network error message.
   E.3 End Use Case.

F. Unknown system error (Step 8)
   F.1 An unexpected error occurs while querying canteen information.
   F.2 The system logs the error.
   F.3 The system returns a generic error message.
   F.4 End Use Case.Priority:MediumFrequency of Use:LowBusiness Rules:? Permission code must remain unique.
? Only admin can update permissions.
? Update action is audited via `auditLogger('UPDATE','Permission','Permission')`.Other Information:Updating the code may affect authorization checks that rely on permission codes.Assumptions:The admin user understands the impact of changing permission codes.
UC41.5 Assign/remove Permission to Role

UC ID and Name:UC41.5_Assign/remove Permission to RoleCreated By:ThaiTQCE181618Date Created:24/3/2026Primary Actor:Admin SystemSecondary Actors:NoneTrigger:The admin user wants to grant or revoke permissions for a specific role.Description:This Use Case allows the Admin System to manage which permissions belong to a role. The UI typically presents a list of permissions with toggle switches and then persists changes by calling assignment APIs.Preconditions:? The actor is logged in.
? The actor has admin privileges.
? At least one role exists.
? At least one permission exists (otherwise there is nothing to assign).Postconditions:? The role-permission mappings are updated according to the admin’s selection.
? The updated permission set for the role can be retrieved via GET `/:roleId/permissions`.Normal Flow:1. The actor opens Permission Management screen.
2. The actor selects a role from the role dropdown.
3. The UI sends GET `/api/roles/:roleId/permissions` to load currently assigned permissions.
4. The system returns the assigned permissions for the role.
5. The UI marks toggles as ON/OFF based on current assignments.
6. The actor changes toggles to add/remove permissions.
7. The actor clicks “Save”.
8. For each permission newly added, the UI sends POST `/api/roles/assign-permission` with `{ roleId, permissionId }`.
9. For each permission removed, the UI sends DELETE `/api/roles/remove-permission/:roleId/:permissionId`.
10. The system updates `RolePermission` mappings accordingly and returns success.
11. The UI refreshes the role’s permission list and shows a success message.Alternative Flows:A. No changes to save (Step 7)
   A.1 The actor clicks Save but the selection is identical to the original.
   A.2 The UI informs “No changes to save”.
   A.3 End Use Case.
B. Duplicate role-permission assignment (Step 8–10)
   B.1 The UI attempts to assign a permission that is already assigned.
   B.2 The system rejects the create due to the unique compound index (duplicate key error).
   B.3 The UI displays error and keeps the edit form open.
C. Remove mapping not found (Step 9–10)
   C.1 The UI attempts to remove an assignment that does not exist.
   C.2 The system returns 404 Not Found (“Role-Permission assignment not found”).
   C.3 The UI refreshes the current assignments to reconcile state.
D. Selected role is deleted during editing (Step 3–10)
   D.1 The role no longer exists.
   D.2 The system returns 404 for role-based queries/updates.
   D.3 The UI refreshes role list and asks the actor to select another role.Exceptions:E. Network connection lost (Step 8-9)
   E.1 Network connection is lost while sending the request.
   E.2 The system/UI displays a network error message.
   E.3 End Use Case.

F. Unknown system error (Step 10)
   F.1 An unexpected error occurs while querying canteen information.
   F.2 The system logs the error.
   F.3 The system returns a generic error message.
   F.4 End Use Case.Priority:HighFrequency of Use:ModerateBusiness Rules:? Only admin can assign/remove permissions for roles.
? A role-permission pair must be unique (compound unique index).
? Assign/remove actions are audited via `auditLogger('UPDATE','Role','Role')`.Other Information:This UC is the basis for `requirePermission(...)` checks in the authorization middleware.Assumptions:The permission catalog is defined and roles exist in the system.
